# Meal Companion audit

Date: 2026-09-13  
Repo: /Users/zero-suminc./projects/clients/Sasha/meal-companion  
HEAD: 3ca35cbb6d46b88b2633cfdff68dabea3feaa13d (main, clean tree)

Finder dimensions: core-rules, sync-data, auth-security, silent-failures, catalog-logging, coach-ai, ui-pwa, tests-ci, health-ingest-oura, expenditure-tdee-weekly-proposals, barcode-scan-diet-safety, export-backup-migration-integrity.  
Finder rounds: 4 (fresh findings per round: r1=41, r2=35, r3=32, r4=28).  
Verification: three adversarial refuters per finding (correctness, reproduction, impact) at xhigh effort. A finding is kept when two of three did not refute it. Severity is the worst corrected severity from the confirming refuters.  

**Result: 98 confirmed, 38 refuted.** Confirmed by severity: 12 critical, 42 high, 39 medium, 5 low.

Note: the loop was stopped after round 4 on the user's instruction. Every round was still producing fresh findings, so this list is not exhaustive. Section 3 lists what a fifth round should cover.

## 1. Confirmed findings

### 1. [critical] bannedReason misses buttermilk and malt (dairy/gluten false negatives)

- **Location:** `lib/foods.ts:148`
- **Evidence:** The gluten check `/wheat|\bbread\b|pasta|barley|\brye\b|couscous|seitan|flour tortilla/` (line 146-147) has no 'malt' term, and the dairy check `/\bmilk\b|cheese|yogurt|yoghurt|\bbutter\b|\bghee\b|whey|casein|cream\b|ice cream/` (line 148-149) uses `\bmilk\b`/`\bbutter\b` word-boundary patterns that do not match inside a compound word with no internal boundary. Verified directly: `bannedReason("Buttermilk")` -> null, `bannedReason("Buttermilk pancakes")` -> null, `bannedReason("Malt vinegar")` -> null, `bannedReason("Malt extract")` -> null. This same function is the sole gate for two live write paths: components/AddFoodSheet.tsx:123 (`canSave = ... && !banned`, a hard block only when bannedReason is non-null) and app/api/mcp/route.ts:92-93 (the AI coach's add-food tool: `const reason = bannedReason(name); if (reason) return text('Refused: ...')`).
- **Scenario:** Sasha (or the AI coach, on her behalf) types 'Buttermilk pancakes' or 'Malt extract' into 'Something else' (AddFoodSheet) or has the coach add it via the MCP tool. bannedReason returns null, the Add button is enabled, and the food is saved with status 'eats' — it is then eligible for eatsByRole/rotationPool (lib/foods.ts:193-202, 321-347) and can be served to her in a real meal.
- **Impact:** This is the one custom-entry safety gate for her colitis constraints (no gluten, no dairy). A false negative here is a direct path for a dairy or gluten item to enter her Eats list and then appear in her daily plan — the exact failure the four core-rule tests exist to prevent, but on a path (custom entry) none of those tests exercise.
- **Proposed fix:** Add 'malt' to the gluten pattern; change the dairy 'milk'/'butter' checks to match as a substring (or explicitly enumerate common compounds like buttermilk) rather than requiring a word boundary that a compound word defeats.
- **Test gap:** lib/foods.test.ts:43-51 only exercises 'Whey isolate', 'Butter', 'Greek yogurt', 'Sourdough bread' — no compound-word case (buttermilk) and no malt case at all.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Confirmed by direct execution and code tracing. ### 2. [critical] Legacy days/foods sync is whole-record last-write-wins, not field-merged

- **Location:** `lib/server/db.ts:86`
- **Evidence:** lib/server/db.ts:86-99 pushRecords' `days` branch: `const rows = body.days.filter((d) => !current.has(d.date) || newer(d.updatedAt, current.get(d.date)?.updatedAt)).map(...); await db().from("days").upsert(rows);` — the entire StoredDay row (extras, eaten, notes, swaps, gut, floorDay, waterMl) is replaced wholesale whenever the incoming updatedAt is later than the server's; there is no per-field merge and no optimistic-concurrency check (contrast with the new engine's apply_sync_op, which uses pg_advisory_xact_lock + base_rev compare-and-swap in supabase/migrations/20260919090300_phase3_sync_entities.sql:399-486). The identical pattern exists for `foods` at lib/server/db.ts:71-84 and `settings` at lib/server/db.ts:101-112.
- **Scenario:** The live, documented MCP integration (docs/codex-setup.md, 'Log my gut as iffy today') calls the `log_gut` tool (app/api/mcp/route.ts:170-186) or `log_food` tool (app/api/mcp/route.ts:207-244), each of which does `const { days } = await pullAll(); const day = { ...(days[d] ?? {date:d}), gut: value (or extras: [...]), updatedAt: new Date().toISOString() }; await pushRecords({ days: [day] });`. Meanwhile Sasha's phone runs the legacy engine on every save (components/SyncBootstrap.tsx:22-24 still calls `syncNow()` from lib/sync.ts alongside the new `syncOnce()`, per the comment 'Legacy engine stays wired until integration Task I-3 deletes lib/sync.ts'). Any local interaction with the SAME calendar day — ticking a meal eaten, adding a food, setting a note, toggling floor day — goes through lib/store.ts:184-189 `updateDay`, which unconditionally rebuilds the WHOLE StoredDay from the phone's own (possibly stale) cached copy and stamps `updatedAt: new Date().toISOString()` on it, synchronously, before any network round trip. The 1.5s-debounced sync (lib/sync.ts:130-171 `syncNow`) then pushes `Object.values(local.days)` (every day, not just the touched one) to /api/sync. Because the local day's updatedAt is stamped at the moment of the tap (essentially always later than the coach's server-side write, which typically happened seconds to minutes earlier), lib/sync.ts:84-97 `mergeSnapshot`'s own pull-then-merge step also rejects the server's (coach's) version as 'not newer', so even the pull-before-push ordering inside one syncNow() cycle does not protect against it. The subsequent push therefore overwrites the server row with the phone's day object, which never contained the coach's addition — the gut check-in or logged meal the coach just wrote is silently and permanently gone, with no conflict, no error, and no trace anywhere in the app.
- **Impact:** Silent, unrecoverable loss of health-tracking data (gut symptom logs, tied directly to her ulcerative colitis monitoring) or logged meals, any time the documented Codex/MCP coach integration and an ordinary phone interaction touch the same day within one sync window. This is exactly the 'lost writes' and 'wipe Sasha's logged days' failure mode this audit round was asked to hunt for, and it sits on the officially sanctioned, currently-live integration path (docs/codex-setup.md), not a hypothetical multi-device scenario.
- **Proposed fix:** Either (a) give lib/server/db.ts pushRecords' `days`/`foods`/`settings` branches a field-level merge (union extras/notes/swaps by key, newer-wins per field, the same shape lib/sync/merge.ts already implements for the new schema) instead of a whole-row replace, or (b) migrate the MCP tools (log_gut, log_food, add_food, set_food_status) off lib/server/db.ts onto the new op-log schema's apply_sync_op RPC, which already has the advisory-lock compare-and-swap this legacy path lacks — this is what 'release B' (the retired PR #18) was presumably meant to do; until one of these lands, the legacy engine should not be run concurrently with routine phone use on the same day.
- **Test gap:** none — AGENTS.md's guarded rules (lib/foods.test.ts, lib/meals.test.ts) cover diet-safety invariants, not sync merge correctness; there is no test anywhere exercising a concurrent legacy-server write racing a phone push for the same StoredDay/food row.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Verified end-to-end, not just at the cited lines. ### 3. [critical] "Log a meal" entries never reach the server log-commit path

- **Location:** `components/LogFoodSheet.tsx:391`
- **Evidence:** saveMeal() (lines 391-419) only calls onAdd({...}) — it never imports or calls commitLogEntry/buildCommitRequest. Contrast with confirmCatalogPortion() (lines 315-352), the ONE path that does commit: `if (mode === "meal") { setItems((list) => [...list, entry]); setQuery(""); setView("meal"); return; }` returns BEFORE reaching `onAdd(entry); const result = await commitLogEntry(...)` a few lines below. confirmPortion() (lines 379-389, the non-catalog bundled/her-list picker still reachable when catalogSearchEnabled() is off) also only calls `onAdd(entry)`, never commitLogEntry. app/page.tsx's logFood() (line 287) that onAdd ultimately reaches just does `addExtra(viewedDate, entry); setLogOpen(null); refresh();` — pure localStorage, lib/store.ts.
- **Scenario:** Sasha taps 'Log a meal', names it 'Chipotle bowl', adds rice + chicken + off-plan queso from the catalog search, saves a note, and taps 'Log this meal'. The entry appears instantly in her Today list and macro rings (all computed locally by lib/resolve.ts from the local extras). No network call is ever made for it: it was written only to lib/store.ts's localStorage-backed `days[date].extras`. The new architecture's server tables (`log_entries`/`log_items`, read by lib/server/log.ts `dayTotalsPrivileged`, which backs the coach's dayTotals/weekTotals executors, the weekly review, and the MCP `get_day` tool) never receive it.
- **Impact:** Every consumer that reads server-side nutrition truth — the AI coach (lib/server/coach.ts's `dayTotals`/`weekTotals` ports), the Sunday weekly review, TDEE/expenditure estimates, and any second device or the Codex/MCP integration — silently undercounts every meal she logs through the primary 'Log a meal' flow, with no error, warning, or log anywhere. For someone managing ulcerative colitis by watching macros and correlating gut symptoms to what she ate, this means the coach and weekly review are reasoning from an incomplete diary specifically missing her multi-food, named meals — the richer, more deliberate entries — while she believes (from her own phone's identical-looking UI) that everything is recorded and synced.
- **Proposed fix:** In saveMeal(), after building the meal entry, call buildCommitRequest/commitLogEntry once per item (or add a meal-level commit) the same way confirmCatalogPortion() does for a single food, before (or alongside) the local onAdd. At minimum, assert in a test that every LogFoodSheet exit path that calls onAdd for a non-legacy item also produces a corresponding outbox/commit call.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Every claim in the finding checks out against the current source. saveMeal() (components/LogFoodSheet.tsx:391-419) and the non-catalog confirmPortion() (:379-389) call only onAdd(...), never buildCommitRequest/commitLogE | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduces. ### 4. [critical] localStorage write failures silently revert every local write

- **Location:** `lib/store.ts:158`
- **Evidence:** saveStore() (lines 158-173): `cached = next;` updates the in-memory store unconditionally, then `try { window.localStorage.setItem(...) } catch { /* silently no-op */ }`. loadStore() (lines 127-144) ignores `cached` and unconditionally re-reads from `window.localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY)` on every call. app/page.tsx's Today() calls `const store = loadStore();` directly in its render body (line 223) on every render.
- **Scenario:** Reproduced standalone (scratchpad/store-repro.mjs mirroring the exact save/load logic): once localStorage.setItem starts throwing (iOS Safari quota, private browsing, storage disabled), toggleEaten()/addExtra()/updateDay() all still call refresh() and the UI shows the tick/log as applied. But the very next render's `loadStore()` call re-reads the untouched (pre-write) localStorage blob and overwrites `cached`, silently reverting the change with no error, no retry and no diagnostic — confirmed: tick survived? false.
- **Impact:** Once local storage starts failing — plausible on an iOS PWA that has accumulated captured-photo drafts, catalog cache, and months of daily logs — every subsequent write (ticking a meal, logging food, floor day, gut/weight check-ins, notes) appears to succeed for an instant and then silently disappears on the next render or app relaunch, with zero signal that anything went wrong. For a one-person health-tracking app this is exactly the kind of failure that quietly erodes trust in the data and can lose a day's gut/symptom log with no way to know it happened.
- **Proposed fix:** Have saveStore() return a success/failure result (or track a `persistFailed` flag) instead of unconditionally updating `cached`; surface a banner/toast on write failure the way DayTypeToggle.tsx already does for its own save path. Add a store.test.ts case that makes localStorage.setItem throw and asserts the next loadStore() does NOT silently discard the pending change.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Re-read lib/store.ts:127-173 and app/page.tsx:151-230 and reproduced the exact scenario against the real (unmodified) module. loadStore() (store.ts:127-144) unconditionally re-reads window.localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KE | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduces exactly as claimed, and is worse than the write-up suggests. I imported the real lib/store.ts (not a mock/mirror) with a fake window.localStorage whose setItem throws, and confirmed: (1) updateDay()->saveStore | impact confirmed (high): Read the real code and reproduced the exact failure end-to-end against the actual module (not a reimplementation). lib/store.ts:158-173 `saveStore()` sets `cached = next` unconditionally, then wraps `localStorage.setItem

### 5. [critical] Catalog search drops the server's ingredient/category policy verdict

- **Location:** `lib/catalog-client.ts:135`
- **Evidence:** lib/server/catalog.ts:163-170 computes `hit.policy = evaluatePolicy(profile, { allergenTags: tags.get(hit.revisionId) ?? null, category: null, onEatsList: ... })` for every search hit, using real ingredient-scanned allergen tags (`allergenTagsByRevision`, built from off-map.ts's `scanAllergens` over the FULL ingredient list, e.g. `whey`, `casein`, `wheat`, `barley`) and her `constraint_profiles` (default `avoid_allergens: ["gluten","dairy","apple"]`, `banned_categories: ["simple_sugars"]`, plus her own `avoid_food_ref_ids`). `FoodSearchHit` (lib/server/catalog.ts:27-32) carries that `policy: PolicyVerdict`. But `toRows()` (lib/catalog-client.ts:135-141) builds `CatalogRow` from the hit and never copies `h.policy` — `CatalogRow` (lib/catalog-client.ts:44-58) has no `policy` field at all. The UI (components/CatalogSearch.tsx `Flags`, components/LogFoodSheet.tsx `offPlanLine`) instead recomputes flags client-side from `offPlanFlags(description + brand)` (lib/catalog-client.ts:66-81), a name-only regex that never reads ingredients, never checks `banned_categories`, and never checks her personal `avoid_food_ref_ids`. By contrast the barcode scan-confirm flow (components/ScanConfirmSheet.tsx:36-46, `badgeFor`) reads `match.policy.verdict === "blocked"` directly and shows "Contains {allergen}", proving the correct UX exists but was never wired into manual catalog search.
- **Scenario:** Sasha searches the catalog for a generic-sounding product, e.g. a store-brand "Trail Mix" or "Protein Bar" whose ingredient list contains wheat flour or whey, or she searches for honey/agave (banned_categories: simple_sugars, a rule the coach added 2026-09-09 specifically to keep off her list). The product's own name/brand text matches none of GLUTEN/DAIRY/APPLE in `offPlanFlags`, so CatalogSearch shows it with no off-plan badge at all. She picks it, sizes a portion, and taps "Log it" believing it's on-plan.
- **Impact:** The app's own stated guarantee ("Off-plan foods are marked, never hidden" — components/LogFoodSheet.tsx:25-28,131; AGENTS.md "A food on Avoid never appears in any date's plan") is silently broken for the most common way she adds new foods (typed search). Combined with the next finding, the entry is added to her visible local log with no warning and is never actually saved server-side — for someone managing ulcerative colitis on a strict no-gluten/no-dairy/no-apple/no-simple-sugar plan, an unflagged gluten or dairy item logged as "fine" is a real safety-relevant miss, not a cosmetic one.
- **Proposed fix:** Add `policy: FoodSearchHit['policy']` to `CatalogRow` and copy `h.policy` through in `toRows()`; have `CatalogSearch`/`LogFoodSheet` prefer the server verdict (same badge `ScanConfirmSheet.badgeFor` already implements) and fall back to the client-side name regex only when offline (`result.kind === "offline"`, which has no server verdict to carry).
- **Test gap:** none

### 6. [critical] Rejected/blocked log commits leave a phantom entry in her visible log with no warning

- **Location:** `components/LogFoodSheet.tsx:349`
- **Evidence:** `confirmCatalogPortion` (components/LogFoodSheet.tsx:315-352) calls `onAdd(entry)` at line 327 — adding the item to her visible Today list — BEFORE awaiting `commitLogEntry`. When the server rejects the commit (e.g. `policyIssues` in lib/server/log.ts:378-394 returns `{status:"invalid", issues:[...]}` because `evaluatePolicy` found a blocked allergen/category), the only handling is `if (result.status !== "ok" && result.status !== "queued") { console.warn("[log] commit rejected", result.status, entryId); }` (lines 349-351) — no toast, no removal of the already-added local entry, no re-open of the sheet. The comment at lines 347-348 says "a rejected commit keeps the entry and says so in the console. Phase 3 turns this into a toast" — but AGENTS.md's phase table records Phase 3 as already landed (PR #41), so this stopgap shipped as final behavior.
- **Scenario:** Continuing the previous scenario (or any catalog food that resolves to a `blocked` policy verdict, e.g. one of her own `avoid_food_ref_ids`): she logs it, the row appears immediately in "Also today" with full macros counted into her kcal/macro rings, but the server never persisted it (commit came back `invalid`). Nothing in the UI tells her this happened — only a `console.warn` she will never see on a phone.
- **Impact:** Her locally-displayed daily totals and "logged" state permanently diverge from what the server actually stored, with zero indication anything went wrong — a phantom entry for a food that was supposed to be blocked specifically to protect her colitis. On any later sync/pull that reconciles from server truth, the entry (and its macros) can silently vanish from a day she already reviewed, or persist indefinitely in the local store since the outbox logic never retries an `invalid` result but also never undoes the optimistic `onAdd`.
- **Proposed fix:** On a non-ok, non-queued `commitLogEntry` result, remove or mark the optimistically-added entry and surface a blocking message to her (the promised Phase 3 toast), especially distinguishing `status: "invalid"` (policy-blocked, must not silently stay logged) from a transient failure.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The finding holds up on direct re-reading of the cited code plus its full call graph. ### 7. [critical] Label-photo allergen scan misses buttermilk/caseinate/milkfat

- **Location:** `lib/capture/propose.ts:34`
- **Evidence:** ALLERGEN_KEYWORDS dairy list is ["milk","cheese","butter","cream","whey","casein","yogurt","yoghurt","lactose","ghee","kefir"], matched with `new RegExp(\`\\b${word}\\b\`)` in allergenTagsFromIngredients (line 45). Compare lib/barcode/off-map.ts:143, the barcode/OFF path's DAIRY regex: `/\b(milk|whey|casein|caseinate|lactose|cheese|yogurt|cream|butterfat|ghee)\b/i` — it explicitly adds "caseinate" and "butterfat" as separate keywords, and tests/barcode/off-map.test.ts:111 asserts `["Sodium caseinate", ["dairy"]]` passes. I ran the two regexes against "buttermilk", "sodium caseinate", and "milkfat" with node: all three return no match under lib/capture/propose.ts's word list, because \b requires a boundary and "buttermilk"/"milkfat" have no space before the substring and "caseinate" is a different whole word than "casein".
- **Scenario:** Sasha photographs the ingredient label of a packaged snack whose printed ingredients read "...natural flavor, sodium caseinate, buttermilk powder..." (common wording on crackers, ranch-flavored chips, and "creamy" baked goods). lib/server/capture.ts's groundItem() (line 209-214) feeds `label.ingredientsText` through `allergenTagsFromIngredients`, which returns `[]` (not null) because none of its dairy keywords match as whole words. lib/policy/constraints.ts's evaluatePolicy (line 36-58) treats a non-null, empty allergenTags array as clean evidence: line 54's `e.allergenTags === null` guard is false, so none of the blocked/unverified branches fire and the function falls through to `{ verdict: "allowed" }` at line 58. The item is shown in her confirm sheet with an "allowed" verdict for a product that in fact contains dairy.
- **Impact:** Sasha has ulcerative colitis and an explicit no-dairy rule (AGENTS.md). The app is telling her a dairy-containing food is allowed to log, based on a keyword matcher the team already knows is too narrow (they hardened the near-identical barcode-path matcher with the same "caseinate"/"butterfat" additions, but never ported that fix to the label-capture path). This happens on the "allowed" branch, which is the one she is least likely to double-check.
- **Proposed fix:** Reuse lib/barcode/off-map.ts's scanAllergens/DAIRY/DAIRY_OK/GLUTEN/GLUTEN_OK regexes (or extract them to a shared module) for lib/capture/propose.ts's allergenTagsFromIngredients, instead of maintaining a second, weaker keyword list. At minimum add "caseinate", "butterfat", and compound-word matches for "buttermilk"/"milkfat" so the two allergen scanners agree.
- **Test gap:** tests/capture/propose.test.ts covers only unambiguous whole-word cases ("apples, milk solids, wheat flour"); it has no case for "buttermilk", "caseinate", or "milkfat", so the divergence from tests/barcode/off-map.test.ts's stronger DAIRY regex was never caught.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Core mechanism is verified real, but the finding overstates its evidence in ways that matter. Confirmed by direct execution: lib/capture/propose.ts's ALLERGEN_KEYWORDS dairy list (line 34-35) has no 'caseinate' or 'butte | reproduction refuted (high): The core code-level claim partially reproduces, but "critical" is not supportable and two of the finding's three headline examples are wrong.

What reproduces: `lib/capture/propose.ts:34-46`'s ALLERGEN_KEYWORDS dairy lis | impact confirmed (high): Every load-bearing claim in the finding checks out against the actual source. lib/capture/propose.ts:34-36 has ALLERGEN_KEYWORDS with the dairy list exactly as quoted, and line 45 does `new RegExp(\`\\b${word}\\b\`)` — c

### 8. [critical] scanAllergens fails open on non-English and vocabulary-gap ingredient text

- **Location:** `lib/catalog/off-map.ts:141`
- **Evidence:** const GLUTEN = /\b(gluten|wheat|barley|rye|spelt|semolina|farro|malt extract)\b/i;
const DAIRY = /\b(milk|whey|casein|caseinate|lactose|cheese|yogurt|cream|butterfat|ghee)\b/i;
const APPLE = /\bapple/i;
Ran these against known-real OFF-style ingredient text: scanAllergens('Farine de blé, lait, beurre, pomme') -> [] (wheat flour, milk, butter, apple -- all three of her banned allergens, zero detected). scanAllergens('Buttermilk') -> []. scanAllergens('Malt syrup') -> []. scanAllergens('Triticale flour') -> [].
- **Scenario:** Sasha scans an OFF-listed product whose submitter filled in ingredients_text but left allergens_tags/traces_tags empty (very common on OFF, and the off-map.ts file header itself says this is why the text scan exists -- 'an OFF pouch with apple juice concentrate ... often declares no allergen at all'). If that ingredient text is in French/German/Spanish (OFF is a French-origin, globally crowdsourced database) or uses an English synonym not in the word lists ('buttermilk', 'malt syrup', 'triticale', 'kefir', 'farina'), scanAllergens returns []. In lib/server/barcode.ts:90-97, ingredients.length > 0 (the ingredient rows exist, they just carry no matched tags), so evidence.allergenTags is [] not null. evaluatePolicy (lib/policy/constraints.ts:36-59) then falls through both the blocked check and the `allergenTags === null` unverified check and returns { verdict: 'allowed' }. ScanConfirmSheet's badgeFor (components/ScanConfirmSheet.tsx:37-48) renders no badge at all for 'allowed', so Sasha sees a clean product screen with no warning and can log or star a product that actually contains gluten, dairy, or apple.
- **Impact:** This is the exact allergen-safety hole the fallback text scan exists to close, and it fails silently on the most common real-world case (non-English or lightly-worded OFF entries) rather than the rare case (missing text) that the code already handles safely. For a UC/no-gluten/no-dairy/no-apple app whose entire purpose is preventing exactly this, a false 'allowed' on a scanned barcode is a direct safety-rule violation, not a cosmetic bug.
- **Proposed fix:** Minimal, targeted fix: don't let a text-scan miss silently become 'allowed'. In ingredientRows/offToRevision, when declaredTags (OFF's structured allergens_tags/traces_tags) is empty, add a quality flag (e.g. 'allergens_text_unconfirmed'); have toMatch's evidence construction treat that flag as allergenTags: null (routes to 'unverified', matching the already-correct no-ingredient-data path) rather than the raw (possibly all-missed) scanAllergens array. Separately, broaden the GLUTEN/DAIRY word lists to include the common misses found above (malt, malt syrup, triticale, farina, buttermilk, kefir) as a defense-in-depth improvement, but the flag-based fix is what actually closes the fail-open gap since no finite English word list will catch every language OFF ships.

### 9. [critical] bannedReason lets common dairy/gluten items through under plausible names

- **Location:** `lib/foods.ts:148`
- **Evidence:** The dairy regex (lib/foods.ts:148) is `/\bmilk\b|cheese|yogurt|yoghurt|\bbutter\b|\bghee\b|whey|casein|cream\b|ice cream/` and the gluten regex (line 146) is `/wheat|\bbread\b|pasta|barley|\brye\b|couscous|seitan|flour tortilla/`. I ran bannedReason against common real-world names: `bannedReason("Feta")`, `bannedReason("Brie wheel")`, `bannedReason("Gouda")`, `bannedReason("Mozzarella sticks")`, `bannedReason("Parmesan")`, `bannedReason("Ricotta")`, `bannedReason("Gruyere")`, `bannedReason("Cheddar shreds")`, `bannedReason("Kefir")`, `bannedReason("Coffee creamer")`, `bannedReason("Orzo")`, `bannedReason("Farro bowl")`, `bannedReason("beer")`, and `bannedReason("soy sauce")` all returned `null` (no refusal) — none contain the literal substrings "cheese", "milk", "butter", "cream", "wheat", "bread", "pasta", etc. that the regex requires.
- **Scenario:** Sasha (or Devin on her behalf) adds a food she wants to eat by typing its real name into the custom-entry path in FoodSheet/AddFoodSheet — "Feta", "Parmesan", "Kefir", "Orzo", or "beer" — expecting the safety gate described in the file header ("Nothing in this file contains gluten, dairy or apple") to refuse it the way it refuses "Cheddar cheese" or "Sourdough bread". It is silently accepted as `status: "eats"`, and from that point on it is eligible to be pulled into her actual meal rotation by rotationPool/composeMeal exactly like any curated seed/preset food.
- **Impact:** This is the one gate whose entire job is enforcing the app's foundational rule ("apple, gluten and dairy never appear in any food") for anything she types by hand rather than picks from the curated seed/preset lists. A bypass here means a genuinely unsafe trigger food for her ulcerative colitis can be added to her real eats list and served to her in a real meal plan, not just miscategorized in a search result.
- **Proposed fix:** Widen the dairy list with common cheese varietals (feta, brie, gouda, mozzarella, parmesan, ricotta, gruyere, cheddar, goat cheese, blue cheese, cottage cheese) plus kefir, quark, creamer, and half-and-half; widen the gluten list with orzo, farro, spelt, semolina, durum, bulgur, cracker, beer, ale, and soy sauce (unless tamari/gluten-free is specified). Better long-term: back this with the same curated allergen/ingredient list already used server-side (lib/catalog/off-map.ts) instead of maintaining a second, narrower hand-written regex.
- **Test gap:** lib/foods.test.ts's bannedReason test only exercises "Cheddar cheese", "Greek yogurt", "Butter", "Whey isolate", "Sourdough bread" — every case already contains a matched substring. No case tests a cheese/gluten item by a name that doesn't literally contain "cheese"/"milk"/"bread"/"wheat".

### 10. [critical] Today-screen writes never reach the new sync engine's outbox

- **Location:** `lib/store.ts:158`
- **Evidence:** `saveStore()` (lib/store.ts:158-172) only writes `localStorage['meal-companion:store:v1']` and calls the single `onSave` listener. Nothing in lib/store.ts imports `lib/sync/outbox.ts` or `lib/sync/idb.ts` (`grep -n "sync|enqueue|outbox|idb" lib/store.ts` returns only comments). app/page.tsx:4 imports `addExtra, getDay, loadStore, removeExtra, saveStore, setSlotNote, updateDay, updateExtra, upsertFood` from `@/lib/store`, and lines 264-341 call these directly to tick a meal eaten (`updateDay(viewedDate, { eaten })`, line 264), toggle Floor Day (270), record swaps (282), add/edit/remove extras (288, 294, 300), set a slot note (305), edit a food (`upsertFood`, 312), and log gut state and weight (336, 341). None of these paths call `enqueue`/`enqueueWithRow` from lib/sync/outbox.ts.
- **Scenario:** Sasha signs in through the app's current (and only) onboarding, `/sign-in` (Supabase Auth, per AGENTS.md 'Auth cut-over ... landed — release A'). She taps to mark breakfast eaten, toggles Floor Day, and logs 'iffy' gut on the Today screen. Each write lands only in this device's localStorage. `lib/sync/outbox.ts`'s `pendingCount()` stays 0 forever for these edits because nothing enqueued a SyncOp for them.
- **Impact:** The core interaction AGENTS.md opens with — 'tells her what to eat and when, lets her tick meals off' — plus Floor Day, swaps, slot notes, manual gut state and weight, and food eats/avoid edits made from the Today screen, is local-storage-only on any device using the current sign-in flow. A lost phone, cleared site data, or a second device loses this history permanently with no server copy, no backup coverage, and no export visibility — a severe data-loss risk for a single-user medical-diet app.
- **Proposed fix:** Wire lib/store.ts's write path (or a thin wrapper around addExtra/updateDay/updateExtra/removeExtra/setSlotNote/upsertFood) to also call the new engine's enqueue/enqueueWithRow against the corresponding synced entities (plan_days, log_entries, gut_log, user_foods), the same way lib/log-client.ts and lib/water-client.ts already do for their flows.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Traced every layer the finding depends on and it holds up, with the gap actually wider than claimed. ### 14. [high] Catalog/scanned foods with macros but no reported calories log as 0 kcal, not "—"

- **Location:** `lib/log-client.ts:37`
- **Evidence:** `MACRO_CODES.kcal` is pinned to the code `"energy_kcal"` (lib/nutrients/codes.ts:25-28), never `"energy_kcal_calc"`. `off-map.ts`'s `offToRevision` (lib/catalog/off-map.ts:198-209) and `fdc-map.ts`'s `fdcBrandedToRevision` (lib/catalog/fdc-map.ts:49-64) only reject an item when ALL FOUR of energy_kcal/protein/carbohydrate/fat_total are null — so a product with real protein/carbs/fat but no `energy-kcal_100g`/`energy_100g` field (common on Open Food Facts crowd data) keeps `per100g.energy_kcal === null` while separately computing `per100g.energy_kcal_calc = atwater.kcal` (a real Atwater estimate) stored under a different nutrient code that is never copied back. `liveStrip` (lib/log-client.ts:33-43) reads `s[code]?.amount ?? 0`, so `at(MACRO_CODES.kcal)` collapses that `null` to `0` — contradicting the app's own explicit rule in lib/nutrients/format.ts:3,13 ("null → '—', never '0'") and the design principle quoted at lib/catalog-client.ts:236-238 ("never a zeroed item"). The same `?? 0` collapse repeats in `snapshotMacros` (lib/resolve.ts:96-107), which is what actually renders her Today rings and `entryMacros` totals from the stored snapshot, and `energyFlags` (lib/catalog/off-map.ts:112-116) explicitly returns `[]` (no quality flag) whenever `reported === null`, so nothing signals the gap anywhere in the UI (FoodDetailSheet correctly shows "—" for the raw per-100g row via `formatAmount`, but that's not the number she acts on or that gets summed).
- **Scenario:** Sasha searches for or scans a store-brand/private-label item whose OFF record lists proteins_100g/carbohydrates_100g/fat_100g but no energy-kcal_100g or energy_100g (a routine gap in OFF's crowd-sourced data). She sizes it to 150 g. `LogFoodPortion`'s confirm button reads "Log it · 150 g · 0 kcal" and the entry commits with real protein/carbs/fat but `kcal: 0`.
- **Impact:** Her calorie total for the day silently under-counts by that food's real calories (often 200-600+ kcal for a serving), with no flag, dash, or warning anywhere in the logging or Today-ring path — for someone whose plan is explicitly calorie/ramp-driven, a systematically wrong number on the single most load-bearing metric in the app, and it looks identical to a correctly-logged zero-calorie item (e.g. black coffee).
- **Proposed fix:** In `liveStrip`/`snapshotMacros` (and the equivalent server-side total), fall back to `energy_kcal_calc` when `energy_kcal` is null before defaulting to 0, mirroring the Atwater fallback the ingestion pipeline already computes but never surfaces; surface a quality flag (or reuse `carb_basis_total`/a new `energy_estimated` flag) so she can see the number is estimated rather than label-verified.
- **Test gap:** none

### 15. [high] Weekly-review job_runs lock is never released on failure

- **Location:** `app/api/jobs/weekly-review/route.ts:44`
- **Evidence:** The route inserts a `job_runs` row with `status: "running"` (lines 44-52) and then calls `writeWeeklyReview` (line 54) with no enclosing try/catch around it; the only catch is the outer `catch (e) { return jsonError(e); }` (lines 59-61), which never updates `job_runs`. Contrast app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts's `takeLock`/`releaseLock` helpers (lines 60-77), which app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts (lines 95-103) and app/api/jobs/weekly-proposal/route.ts (lines 71-89) both wrap in `try { ...; releaseLock(..., "ok", ...) } catch (e) { releaseLock(..., "error", ...) }` — the sibling cron jobs always release their lock, successful or not. No migration adds a TTL/cleanup job for stale `job_runs` rows (grepped supabase/migrations for job_runs cleanup: none found).
- **Scenario:** writeWeeklyReview() calls runWeeklyReview() -> generateStructured(), which throws when the paid Gemini runtime is unavailable — documented as having actually happened in production the same day this code was committed (docs/ai-data-handling.md: "gemini-3.8-flash... exhausted all three application retries with 503 high demand"). When that happens on a Sunday weekly-review cron run, the `job_runs` row for `("weekly-review", userId, weekStart)` is left at `status: "running"` forever (no `finished_at`). Any retry that same week — an automatic Vercel cron retry or a manual re-POST — hits the unique index on `(job, user_id, run_key)` and gets `409 conflict: "a run already holds this week's lock"` from lines 47-52, even though no run is actually in progress.
- **Impact:** One transient AI-provider hiccup permanently blocks Sasha's weekly review for that entire week, with no automatic recovery and no visible error state (the row still reads "running", not "error", so a status query wouldn't even surface it as a failure). This is exactly the "unlogged failure on a main path" pattern the audit asked to hunt for, and it is not hypothetical — the 503 it depends on is already recorded as having occurred that same day.
- **Proposed fix:** Wrap the `writeWeeklyReview` call in the same try/catch-and-release pattern the estimate and weekly-proposal jobs use: on success `update` the row to `status: "ok"`, on failure `update` it to `status: "error"` (with the error message) so the unique-index lock is freed and a retry that week can proceed. Reusing `takeLock`/`releaseLock` from app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts would also fix it and remove the duplicated insert logic.
- **Test gap:** No test in tests/coach/weekly-review.test.ts exercises a writeWeeklyReview failure through the actual route handler (app/api/jobs/weekly-review/route.ts) to check the job_runs row's final status.

### 16. [high] SW cache silently masks true offline state on Body dashboard

- **Location:** `public/sw.js:59`
- **Evidence:** public/sw.js:59-69 `networkFirst()` does `fetch(request).then(putInCache).catch(() => caches.match(request)...)` and line 97 routes every other same-origin GET (no `/api/` exclusion) through it, even though the file's own header comment (lines 4-6) says 'only network responses (HTML, JS/CSS chunks, icons, manifest) are cached here.' Meanwhile lib/health-client.ts:49-63 `fetchDaily()` calls `fetch('/api/health/daily?from=...&to=...', {cache:'no-store'})` and relies on the promise REJECTING to know she's offline (sets `offline:true`, skips `writeCachedDaily`). components/BodyDashboard.tsx:166-167 only renders 'Offline — last synced {lastSeen(fetchedAt)}.' when `offline` is true.
- **Scenario:** Sasha opens /body once while online (SW caches the 200 response for that day's `from`/`to` query, which stays the same all day). Later that day her phone loses signal and she reopens /body. The SW's `fetch()` inside the worker fails, so `networkFirst` falls back to `caches.match(request)` and resolves with the STALE cached 200 response — the page's own `fetch()` never rejects, so `fetchDaily()`'s `catch` block never runs. The code takes the success path: it treats the stale JSON as fresh, calls `writeCachedDaily(response)` which overwrites `fetchedAt` with `now`, and returns `offline:false`.
- **Impact:** Sasha sees old weight/sleep/expenditure numbers stamped with a fresh-looking timestamp and no 'Offline — last synced' banner, even though `lib/health-client.ts` was specifically built to show that banner. She has no way to tell the numbers are stale, which matters for a health dashboard she uses to judge her ramp/TDEE state.
- **Proposed fix:** Exclude `/api/` (or same-origin fetch requests with an `Accept: application/json` header) from the SW's cache-and-serve fallback — let those requests reject/504 on network failure like the `cacheFirst`/`navigate` paths already special-case, so the app-layer offline detection in health-client.ts (and any other JSON fetcher) keeps working as designed.
- **Test gap:** none — no test exercises public/sw.js's fetch handler against an authenticated JSON route
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The finding's mechanism checks out end-to-end against the actual code. public/sw.js:75-97 registers one `fetch` listener for every same-origin GET; the only carve-outs are `mode === "navigate"` (line 82), `/_next/static/ | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduced directly. ### 30. [high] Oura samples hardcode tz='UTC', shifting sleep/RHR/HRV to the wrong local day

This is inconsistent with the rest of the codebase's single-user timezone convention: `DEFAULT_TZ = "America/New_York"` (lib/health/contract.ts:18) is the value used everywhere else that needs a fallback zone — lib/server/health-ingest.ts:57 for Health Auto Export payloads, and app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts:26/87 and app/api/jobs/weekly-proposal/route.ts:23/67 for the nightly jobs. Because bedtime_start/bedtime_end are real UTC-offset instants (not synthetic midnight placeholders), any night Sasha goes to bed between 8 PM and midnight Eastern — i.e. virtually every night — has her sleep_asleep, sleep_in_bed, resting_heart_rate and hrv_sdnn samples from that sleep document bucketed one calendar day later than her real local night. DEFAULT_TZ` to match lib/server/health-ingest.ts:57), so every Oura sample is bucketed into the same local calendar day as her phone-sourced samples.

### 32. [high] job_runs lock has no error-retry path — a clean job failure blocks the whole week

- **Location:** `app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts:60`
- **Evidence:** export async function takeLock(
  admin: ReturnType<typeof adminClient>, job: string, userId: string, runKey: string,
): Promise<boolean> {
  const { error } = await admin.from("job_runs")
    .insert({ job, user_id: userId, run_key: runKey, status: "running" }).select("job");
  if (!error) return true;
  if ((error as { code?: string }).code === "23505") return false;
  throw error;
}
-- job_runs primary key (supabase/migrations/20261006090100_phase6_expenditure.sql:36-40) is (job, user_id, run_key) with NO status column in the key, so ANY existing row — 'running', 'ok', or a cleanly-finished 'error' — makes the next insert conflict identically.
- **Scenario:** Sunday's weekly-proposal cron takes the lock for (weekly-proposal, sasha, weekStart), runs estimate successfully, then throws inside writeProposal (e.g. the write_target_proposal RPC times out, or activeProfile hits a transient Supabase error). The catch block in app/api/jobs/weekly-proposal/route.ts:85-88 correctly calls releaseLock(..., "error", ...), leaving a clean, finished job_runs row with status='error'. Nothing retries that (job,user,week) key automatically, and any later POST to /api/jobs/weekly-proposal for the same week — an automatic platform retry, or a manual re-trigger by Devin after spotting the error — has takeLock hit the same primary-key conflict and silently returns 'skipped', never calling runEstimate or buildProposal again.
- **Impact:** A single transient failure permanently forfeits that week's proposal with no self-healing and no code path distinguishing 'already handled, don't redo' from 'failed, please retry' — directly contradicting the route's own doc comment ('Idempotent on (user_id, week_start) through both the job_runs lock and the unique index'). Devin has no visibility unless he manually queries job_runs, and Sasha simply gets no target update that week.
- **Proposed fix:** Have takeLock (or a wrapper used by the weekly jobs) treat a pre-existing row with status='error' as reclaimable — e.g. delete/replace it before inserting, or check status in the conflict branch and only refuse when status is 'running' or 'ok'.
- **Test gap:** tests/expenditure/jobs.test.ts's 'a same-week retry calls the RPC again' tests use a lockOk() mock that ALWAYS accepts the insert and explicitly say they do not verify real job_runs semantics ('the SQL's week_start exclusion, not this mock, is what leaves the existing pending row untouched') — no test exercises a retry after a status='error' row, real or mocked.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (medium): The core mechanism is confirmed: job_runs' primary key is (job, user_id, run_key) with status only a CHECK-constrained column, not part of the key, in BOTH the table's real origin (20260926000000_phase5_oura_oauth.sql:10 | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduced directly against the real code. All cited facts check out: job_runs' primary key is (job, user_id, run_key) with status only inside a CHECK constraint, not the key (supabase/migrations/20261006090100_phase6_ex | impact refuted (high): The mechanics are real (job_runs PK (job,user_id,run_key) has no status differentiation, so an 'error' row blocks any later insert for the same key with the same 23505-swallowing takeLock at app/api/jobs/estimate/route.t

### 35. [high] A 'gone' op result is silently dropped, leaving the local mirror row untouched

- **Location:** `lib/sync/outbox.ts:277`
- **Evidence:** applyResult (lib/sync/outbox.ts:259-292): only "applied", "conflict", "deleted", "unsupported" and "error" get a branch; everything else — including "gone" — falls through to `return dropOp(op); // 'gone' and 'duplicate': the op has nothing left to do` (line 291). `dropOp` (lines 301-304) for a non-SETTINGS entity is just `await deleteOp(op.op_id)` — it never calls `putRow`/`deleteRow` on the entity's mirror store. Contrast with the "deleted" branch (lines 264-276), which does call `deleteRow`/`clearPulledUserSettings` to bring the local mirror back in line with server truth. `drainOutbox` (line 349) buckets a 'gone' result into `out.dropped`, never `out.errored`, so `out.failed` stays false; `syncOnce` (lib/sync/client.ts:167-172) then calls `setLastError(null)` and `setLastSyncAt(...)` — a full success signal. The outbox test at lib/sync/outbox.test.ts:146-149 only asserts `pendingCount()` reaches 0 after a 'gone' result; it never checks the mirror row or the reported error state, so this hole is untested as well as unhandled.
- **Scenario:** Any offline-queued `update` or `delete` op for `user_foods`, `log_entries`, `gut_log`, `recipes`, `favorites_*`, `plan_days`, `water_log`, `user_food_prefs`, or `duplicate_groups` whose entity_id the server has no row for at all (not tombstoned — genuinely never created there, e.g. because an earlier 'create' op for the same entity was itself lost to this exact same code path, or the phone's IndexedDB outbox and mirror drifted apart after a crash between two separate writes) gets `{status:'gone'}` back from `apply_sync_op` (supabase/migrations/20260919090300_phase3_sync_entities.sql:457-459). The client deletes the op and does nothing else. Concretely: she edits a custom food's allergen note while offline (an `update` op queued against a food whose original `create` never actually reached the server), later reconnects — the update op comes back 'gone', is silently discarded, `lastSyncAt`/`lastError` report success, and her edited food/log entry sits in local IndexedDB forever, never reaching the server and never reaching any other device.
- **Impact:** Silent, permanent data loss on a main sync path with a false 'everything is sent' signal (SyncStatus reads exactly this state — see components/SyncStatus.tsx:38-42, 'Everything is sent'). For a phone-first PWA that is the sole record of her food log and allergen notes across devices, a write that never left this phone and is never flagged as failed is unrecoverable — she has no reason to suspect it, and nothing in the app ever retries or surfaces it.
- **Proposed fix:** In applyResult, give 'gone' its own branch: for an update/create, re-derive the row from the local op's payload and re-queue it as a fresh `create` op (base_rev: null) instead of dropping it; for a delete, since the server already has nothing, just delete the local mirror row to match. Either way, increment a counter that marks the drain as failed/needs-attention so SyncStatus does not report success, and log it via setLastError so she has a chance to notice and retry.
- **Test gap:** lib/sync/outbox.test.ts's "drops the op on 'gone' and on 'duplicate'" test (line 146) only asserts `pendingCount()` — add an assertion on the mirror row surviving/being repaired and on `DrainResult.failed`/`errored` to catch this.

### 39. [high] A catalog item with macros but unmeasured kcal drops its calories from Today's totals, not just the log sheet

- **Location:** `lib/resolve.ts:101`
- **Evidence:** `snapshotMacros()` (lib/resolve.ts:96-118) treats an item as 'measured' whenever `Object.keys(i.snapshot).length > 0` (lib/resolve.ts:92-94, `measuredItems`), then reads each macro via `at(code) => totals.get(code)?.amount ?? 0` (line 101). `totalsFor()` (lib/nutrients/totals.ts:24-45) adds a nutrient code to the map the moment any item carries that key in its snapshot, regardless of whether `value.amount` is null (lines 33-40: when `value.amount === null` the row is still created/kept with `amount: 0`, only `itemsWithValue` is skipped). So an item whose per-100g `energy_kcal` is genuinely unmeasured (null — a real, valid state under architecture §3.1, common for FDC Branded/OFF rows that report protein/carbs/fat but not a parsed calorie value) still counts as 'measured' overall because its protein/carbs/fat ARE present, and its contribution to `kcal` collapses to 0 via the `?? 0` fallback. `entryMacros()` (line 121) returns this silently-zeroed total, and `resolveDay()` (line 165: `extras.reduce((acc, e) => add(acc, entryMacros(e)), fromSlots)`) folds it straight into the day's `consumed` macros — the numbers driving Today's rings, the weekly proposal/TDEE inputs, and the coach's day context — not just the one-time display on the log sheet.
- **Scenario:** She logs a branded protein bar via catalog search where the source has protein_g/carbohydrate_g/fat_total present but no energy_kcal value. The entry is 'measured' (has a non-empty snapshot), so it is never treated as an unmeasured/manual entry that would carry its own stored `macros.kcal`. Its 200+ real calories are silently omitted from every kcal total for that day: the day's consumed-kcal ring reads low, the remaining-budget number looks more generous than it is, and any weekly TDEE/proposal calculation built on `resolveDay` inherits the same undercount.
- **Impact:** This is a distinct, wider-blast-radius version of the already-known 'catalog item with no reported calories shows 0 kcal, not —' display bug (lib/log-client.ts:37 covers only the one-time confirm-sheet strip): here the same `?? 0` collapse happens inside the shared totals pipeline that resolveDay, the Today rings, and every other reader of `entryMacros`/`resolveDay` depend on, so a single logged item with this gap corrupts her whole day's calorie total silently, every time that day is resolved — not just at the moment she logs it.
- **Proposed fix:** In `snapshotMacros`, only treat kcal (and each macro) as present when its snapshot `amount` is non-null for at least one item; otherwise fall back to the food's Atwater-computed `energy_kcal_calc` (already stored per lib/catalog/off-map.ts:112-116 / fdc-map.ts:61-64) instead of silently substituting 0, and surface a coverage/estimate marker on the day total the way `dayNutrientTotals`/`microCoverage` already do for micros.
- **Test gap:** lib/resolve.test.ts / lib/nutrients/totals.test.ts (none found asserting a mixed-measured item — protein/carbs/fat present, energy_kcal null — still contributes its true calories, or at least is excluded rather than zeroed, in `snapshotMacros`/`resolveDay`'s consumed totals)
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Traced the full pipeline and reproduced it with a live run of the actual project code (tsx, no repo files modified). computeSnapshot (lib/nutrients/snapshot.ts:90-112) is always called with a per100g map from emptyPer100 | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduced end-to-end with the actual production code (no mocks) via a tsx script that imports lib/nutrients/snapshot.ts, lib/catalog/off-map.ts, lib/nutrients/totals.ts, and lib/resolve.ts directly. The same mechanism blocks an Oura re-pull that legitimately revises a sleep or readiness score after Oura's own backend finishes processing (oura/map.ts's `sample()` reuses the identical `id:type` vendor_sample_id on every pull), and blocks a corrected step_count, resting_heart_rate or body_mass reading from HAE the same way.

Because lib/server/health-daily.ts's mergedDays/rollUpDay is the one read path for the dashboard, the MCP tool and the Phase 6 TDEE/expenditure estimator, this feeds systematically undercounted (or otherwise stale) energy and activity numbers into everything Sasha and the coach see about her body, with no error anywhere — the ingest response even reports these as 'updated' successes at the API layer conceptually, but the DB write is actually a silent skip.
- **Proposed fix:** Either (a) have normalizeHealthAutoExport and lib/oura/map.ts's sample() populate vendor_updated_at from the source's own freshness signal (HAE's payload `sent_at`/receipt time for HAE rows, the Oura document's `updated_at`/pull time for Oura rows) so the existing WHERE clause can actually fire, or (b) drop the vendor_updated_at gate and let a same-dedupe-key re-send unconditionally overwrite value/unit/end_time (accepting last-write-wins, which is safe here since a resend for the same source+key is always a refinement of the same observation, not a stale replay — that's already handled separately by the payload-hash/event dedup layer).

### 49. [high] commitLogEntry swallows queue() rejection only structurally, not really

- **Location:** `lib/log-client.ts:135`
- **Evidence:** async function commitLogEntry(...) { try { const res = await doFetch(...); if (res.status >= 500 || RETRY_STATUSES.has(res.status)) return queue(req); return (await res.json())...; } catch { return queue(req); } }  (lines 129-139); queue() calls `await enqueue(...)` -> `appendOp` -> `openSmcDb()`, which explicitly documents 'A rejected promise must not be cached, or one failed open wedges sync for the whole session' (lib/sync/idb.ts:41) and lets the open failure propagate. Verified empirically: `return innerAsyncCall()` inside a try (without `await`) does NOT get caught by the surrounding catch in Node/V8 — a script reproducing this exact shape (`return inner()` unawaited inside try/catch) resolved as REJECTED at the caller, bypassing the catch entirely.
- **Scenario:** Sasha is offline or IndexedDB is unavailable (private browsing, storage quota hit, or a blocked DB upgrade — all real states on iOS Safari, the PWA's primary platform). She logs a food from the main 'Log a food' sheet. commitLogEntry's fetch fails (offline) -> `return queue(req)` is taken -> queue() calls enqueue() -> appendOp() -> openSmcDb() rejects. Because `return queue(req)` was never awaited, that rejection is NOT caught by commitLogEntry's own try/catch and propagates straight out of commitLogEntry as a rejected promise.
- **Impact:** Three primary, daily-used call sites (`components/LogFoodSheet.tsx:328` confirmCatalogPortion, `components/ScanConfirmSheet.tsx:116` logIt, `components/CaptureSheet.tsx:190/241` commitAndReport/save via lib/capture-client.ts:245 commitDraft) all `await commitLogEntry(...)`/`commitDraft(...)` with no matching catch (LogFoodSheet has none at all; ScanConfirmSheet has only try/finally with no catch; CaptureSheet's save() has no try/catch either). In LogFoodSheet the food is already optimistically added to the visible day (`onAdd(entry)` at line 327, BEFORE the commit call) — so Sasha sees the meal logged on her Today screen while it was neither committed to the server NOR queued in the outbox: silent data loss on her core food-logging path, with an unhandled promise rejection as the only trace. In CaptureSheet, `setSaving(false)` never runs (no finally), leaving the Save button stuck in a saving state forever with no error message.
- **Proposed fix:** Await the returned promise before returning it inside both branches of commitLogEntry (`return await queue(req);` in both the try and catch bodies), or wrap queue(req) in its own try/catch that degrades to a clearly failed status instead of a silent local success. Then ensure every call site (LogFoodSheet.confirmCatalogPortion, ScanConfirmSheet.logIt, CaptureSheet.save/commitAndReport) catches the rejection and surfaces an error state instead of leaving an optimistic entry with no backing write.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The core JS/TS mechanism is real and I reproduced it directly: an unawaited `return innerAsyncCall()` inside a try block is NOT caught by that block's own catch when the inner promise rejects — Node confirmed REJECTED, n | reproduction confirmed (high): The core mechanics are real and reproducible, but "critical" overstates the impact, and one part of the causal story (the offline scenario) is mischaracterized.

She taps the grams stepper's minus button repeatedly (each tap is only 5 g, or she overshoots while correcting a wrong estimate) until it reads 0 g, then taps Save without removing the row. applyEdits keeps the item (removed:false), canSave passes (foodRefId set, verdict not blocked), and commitDraft sends `{ grams: 0, qty: 0, unit: "g", ... }` for that item. Both the shared zod schema and the log_items CHECK constraint accept it, so the entry is written and appears in her log as "Rice · 0 g · 0 kcal · 0P 0C 0F" (components/LoggedExtras.tsx:97-98 renders whatever entryMacros() returns, with no floor and no distinct treatment for a zero-quantity entry).
- **Impact:** A real food she believes she logged silently contributes nothing to her daily totals (kcal/macros/fiber all read 0), while looking exactly like a normal, successfully-logged item — no warning, no block, no distinguishing mark (unlike the deliberate 'marking, never hiding' off-plan pattern used everywhere else in this codebase). For a colitis patient whose whole app exists to keep an honest log against fixed macro targets, a phantom zero-calorie entry that looks legitimate is a data-integrity failure exactly of the kind the rest of the log-commit contract goes out of its way to prevent (e.g. rejecting negative grams, rejecting an entry with zero items).
- **Proposed fix:** Require grams > 0 (matching recipe_items' own check) in logItemInputSchema and in the log_items CHECK constraint, and add a client-side floor: CaptureConfirm's Stepper floor should be a small positive minimum (or the row should auto-remove / block Save when grams hits 0), and lib/capture-client.ts's canSave should also require every kept item to have grams > 0.

### 54. [high] Missed/late weekly-proposal cron permanently skips that week's check-in

- **Location:** `app/api/jobs/weekly-proposal/route.ts:67`
- **Evidence:** const asOf = localDateIn(DEFAULT_TZ);
    const weekStart = mondayOf(asOf);
    ... (lines 67-68) — asOf is always 'now', read at the moment the handler executes; there is no parameter to target a specific week/date. mondayOf(asOf) (app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts:35-41) derives weekStart purely from that wall-clock date.
- **Scenario:** The Sunday 21:00-local cron invocation fails to run or errors before reaching writeProposal (Vercel outage, ESTIMATOR_ENABLED momentarily unset, a transient DB error, the global enforceRateLimit('jobs:weekly-proposal', 10, 3600) briefly saturated). Devin notices the next day (Monday/Tuesday) and re-triggers the endpoint by hand with the cron secret. asOf now resolves to Monday's date, so mondayOf(asOf) returns the CURRENT week's Monday, not the Sunday-just-passed week that actually needed a proposal. The job runs 'successfully' but computes and writes a proposal (or 'none') for the wrong week; the week whose Sunday check-in was missed never gets a proposal at all, silently and permanently — no error, no job_runs row, no alert names the gap.
- **Impact:** Sasha relies on the Sunday proposal as her weekly opportunity to adjust calories/macros in step with her actual expenditure trend (weight loss/gain, training changes). A silently-skipped week means a week goes by with no chance to correct an outdated target, and nothing in the product — not Devin, not Sasha, not any log — ever surfaces that the check-in was missed rather than 'nothing warranted a change.'
- **Proposed fix:** Compute weekStart from an explicit, persisted 'last week we ran for' pointer (e.g., the max week_start already present in target_proposals/expenditure_estimates, or a dedicated job_runs high-water-mark) rather than solely from wall-clock 'now', and accept an optional target-week override on manual retries so a missed Sunday can be backfilled instead of silently absorbed into whichever week happens to contain the retry.
- **Test gap:** tests/expenditure/jobs.test.ts covers mondayOf's date arithmetic and same-day retry idempotency, but has no case for 'job invoked on a later day than its intended cron week' — the exact scenario that produces a silently skipped week.

### 55. [medium] Server ramp-stage/profile date uses UTC, diverging from her local day

- **Location:** `lib/server/targets.ts:27`
- **Evidence:** `function today(): string { return new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10); }` (lines 27-29) returns the UTC calendar date, then feeds both `activeProfile`'s `.lte("effective_from", today())` filter (line 35) and `activeStage`'s `rampStageFor(firstOpenedAt, today(), override)` (line 48). rampStageFor (lib/plan.ts:70-84) treats its `date` argument as a bare 'yyyy-mm-dd' calendar day with no timezone, exactly like the client does — but the client derives that string from the phone's own local clock, while the server derives it from UTC. Any US timezone is behind UTC, so for several hours around each local midnight the server computes 'today' as one calendar day ahead of Sasha's actual day.
- **Scenario:** Sasha opens the coach or app on day 7 of the ramp in the evening, local time (already past midnight UTC). The server's activeStage()/floorsFor() computes rampStageFor with a date one day ahead, landing on the day-7/8 boundary a day early and returning ramp stage 2's protein floor instead of stage 1's, while the phone (still local day 7) shows stage 1 targets — the two disagree on the same real day. The same skew can pick a target_profiles row whose `effective_from` is 'tomorrow' UTC up to ~8 hours before it is actually tomorrow for her.
- **Impact:** This is a 'date edge case: timezone on phone vs server' with a real numeric consequence — the server-computed protein floor (used to validate/clamp any target write) and the active target profile can silently disagree with what she sees on the phone, right at the ramp-stage boundaries the tests exist to guard.
- **Proposed fix:** Pass the client's local calendar date (already sent for other endpoints) into activeStage/activeProfile instead of computing 'today' from server UTC time, or store/require an explicit timezone for the boundary computation.
- **Test gap:** lib/plan.test.ts's rampStageFor tests (lines 46-86) all run in one implicit process timezone and never simulate a server-UTC-vs-client-local divergence; there is no test for lib/server/targets.ts's today() at all.

### 56. [medium] Spoofable X-Forwarded-For used as rate-limit key on ~11 routes

- **Location:** `app/api/log/propose/route.ts:22`
- **Evidence:** app/api/log/propose/route.ts:22  `const ip = req.headers.get("x-forwarded-for")?.split(",")[0]?.trim() ?? "unknown"; await enforceRateLimit(`log:propose:ip:${ip}`, 30, 60);`. The same pattern (first hop of x-forwarded-for as the rate-limit key, applied BEFORE requireUser) appears in app/api/targets/route.ts:28, app/api/log/commit/route.ts:29, app/api/export/route.ts:165, app/api/expenditure/route.ts:12, app/api/proposals/route.ts:15, app/api/proposals/[id]/decide/route.ts:23, app/api/media/sign/route.ts:18, and lib/server/catalog.ts:68-72 (`clientIp`, shared by app/api/foods/search/route.ts, app/api/foods/barcode/route.ts, app/api/foods/[id]/route.ts). Contrast with app/auth/callback/route.ts:18-30, whose own comment explains why: "Vercel's edge network sets x-real-ip to the address it actually accepted the connection from and overwrites any client-supplied value, so it can be trusted. x-forwarded-for cannot: a caller can set it to anything, so keying the limiter on it lets an attacker rotate past enforceRateLimit entirely." That route uses `req.headers.get("x-real-ip")` instead. lib/server/catalog.test.ts:47-51 only tests that the first hop is parsed correctly, never that it resists spoofing.
- **Scenario:** An unauthenticated caller sends repeated POST /api/log/propose (or /api/coach, /api/export, /api/media/sign, etc.) requests, setting a different `X-Forwarded-For: <random-ip>` header on every request. Because Vercel appends the real client IP after any client-supplied value rather than replacing it, `.split(",")[0]` always reads the attacker-controlled value, so each request lands in a fresh `enforceRateLimit` bucket and the pre-identity IP throttle (e.g. 30/60s on log:propose, 120/60s on coach:pre) never engages. The attacker can drive unlimited traffic into `requireUser()` (a live call to Supabase's auth/JWKS service) and into AI-call-adjacent routes, with no bound from the IP leg of the design's own two-layer (IP + user) rate limiting.
- **Impact:** This defeats the pre-identity "flood brake" the codebase's own comments describe as load-bearing (e.g. coach route: "this first limiter is the pre-identity flood brake"; log/propose: "burst 30/min"). For a phone-first PWA serving one person, sustained spoofed-header flooding can drive up Supabase Auth/API cost or trip Supabase's own account-level throttling, degrading or blocking the app exactly when Sasha needs to log a meal or check her plan — and it undermines a control the team explicitly engineered correctly once (auth/callback) but did not carry to the other 8+ places that need it.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace `req.headers.get("x-forwarded-for")?.split(",")[0]` with `req.headers.get("x-real-ip")` (falling back to a per-request unique key when absent, as app/auth/callback/route.ts:29-32 already does) in every route listed above, and in `clientIp()` in lib/server/catalog.ts so foods/search, foods/barcode and foods/[id] inherit the fix. Centralizing this as one shared helper (e.g. exported from lib/server/http.ts) would prevent the inconsistency from recurring.
- **Test gap:** lib/server/catalog.test.ts (clientIp) and each route's own *.test.ts cover IP-key parsing but never assert resistance to a spoofed x-forwarded-for value; none of the tests exercise the trust boundary the auth/callback route's own tests do implicitly by using x-real-ip.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Refuted=false: every factual claim in the finding checks out byte-for-byte against the repo. All ~11 call sites use the identical spoofable-XFF pattern before requireUser(); the codebase's own auth/callback route documen | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduces exactly as described. Every cited location uses the identical spoofable pattern `req.headers.get("x-forwarded-for")?.split(",")[0]?.trim() ?? "unknown"` as the IP-scoped rate-limit key, applied before `require | impact refuted (high): The code claim is accurate — every cited route (log/propose:22, targets:28, log/commit:29, export:165, expenditure:12, proposals:15, proposals/[id]/decide:23, media/sign:18, catalog.ts clientIp:68-72) does key the pre-id

### 57. [medium] Legacy /api/sync push has no rate limit, no size cap, no schema validation

- **Location:** `app/api/sync/route.ts:20`
- **Evidence:** app/api/sync/route.ts:12-26 — both GET and POST call only `bearerOk(req, "SASHA_SYNC_TOKEN")` and `isConfigured()`; there is no `enforceRateLimit` call anywhere in the file (every other route in the repo calls `enforceRateLimit` before or alongside identity — e.g. app/api/log/commit/route.ts:29-30, app/api/sync/ops/route.ts). POST also does `const body = (await req.json()) as PushBody;` (line 23) — an unchecked TypeScript cast, not a runtime `zod` parse — versus app/api/sync/ops/route.ts, which validates every op through `OpsRequestSchema`, and every other body-accepting route in the app, which goes through `readJson(req, schema, maxBytes)` (lib/server/http.ts:24-45) for both a size cap and zod validation. lib/server/db.ts:24-28 defines `PushBody` as a bare TS type with no corresponding schema. No app/api/sync/route.test.ts exists (confirmed absent) to guard any of this.
- **Scenario:** AGENTS.md states this legacy sync path is still live in production alongside the new Supabase-auth path ("Phase 0 release A — legacy and new path both live"). Anyone holding SASHA_SYNC_TOKEN (or a buggy/looping client using it) can POST an arbitrarily large JSON body with any shape to /api/sync with no throttle: a malformed `days` entry that happens to satisfy `newer()` (e.g. missing fields `resolveDay` in lib/resolve.ts expects, or a `date` key that doesn't match its own `.date` field) is upserted straight into the `days` table via `pushRecords` (lib/server/db.ts:84-96) with no schema check, and a client that retries aggressively has no per-minute cap bounding how many writes land.
- **Impact:** This is the store for Sasha's entire food list and daily meal history. A malformed or oversized push here risks corrupting `days`/`foods`/`settings` data with no server-side shape guard (unlike every other write path in the app), which is exactly the kind of "any date renders a complete day" guarantee AGENTS.md's own test-guarded rules depend on — a corrupted day record could silently break her plan rendering. It also has no defense-in-depth against a compromised or misbehaving client hammering writes.
- **Proposed fix:** Add `enforceRateLimit` (mirroring the pattern used by every cron job and every other authenticated route) and switch `(await req.json()) as PushBody` to `readJson(req, pushBodySchema, MAX_BYTES)` with a real zod schema for `PushBody`, matching the rigor already applied to the newer app/api/sync/ops path. Given this path is slated for retirement in release B, accelerating that cutover is an acceptable alternative to hardening code being deleted soon.
- **Test gap:** none

- **Proposed fix:** Branch on saveDraft()'s return value in settle(): when it is "too_large" or "unavailable", set a user-visible message (the file already has a `message` state slot used elsewhere) instead of silently continuing as if it persisted.

### 59. [medium] ProposalCard approve/reject has no error handling

- **Location:** `components/ProposalCard.tsx:37`
- **Evidence:** decide() (lines 37-45): `async function decide(decision) { setBusy(true); try { await decideProposalOnline(proposal.id, decision); onDecided(decision); } finally { setBusy(false); } }` — there is no catch. decideProposalOnline() (lib/estimator-client.ts lines 85-100) throws `new Error("Needs a connection")` when offline and `new Error(\`decide failed: ${res.status}\`)` on any non-2xx response.
- **Scenario:** Sasha taps 'Use these' on the Today-screen target proposal card when the request fails (e.g. a transient 500, or the network drops mid-request while `navigator.onLine` still reads true). decideProposalOnline() throws; decide()'s try/finally has no catch, so the rejection propagates unhandled from the onClick handler. `busy` resets to false (button re-enables) but onDecided is never called and no message renders anywhere in the component.

### 60. [medium] Runtime SDK pin (assertPinnedSdk) is never called outside tests/canary

- **Location:** `lib/ai/adapter.ts:37`
- **Evidence:** `assertPinnedSdk` is defined at lib/ai/adapter.ts:37-44 and the adapter's own top-of-file comment claims "The installed SDK major is asserted for the same reason [as the model-id pin]: a major upgrade gets its own canary, deliberately." Grepping the whole repo, the only callers are scripts/ai-canary.ts:77 (a manual, off-CI script) and lib/ai/adapter.test.ts. `generateStructured` (the function every real request path — coach, capture, weekly review — goes through) only calls `installedGenai()` at line 293 to stamp `sdk_version` on the `ai_calls` row; it never calls `assertPinnedSdk()`, so a major-version mismatch is recorded but not blocked at runtime.
- **Scenario:** The only actual enforcement of the SDK-major pin at build time is scripts/verify-ai-runtime-trace.mjs (wired into `pnpm build`, run in CI's `build` job), which compares the installed `@google/genai` package.json against the hand-maintained lib/ai/sdk-version.json. If that build/CI step were ever skipped for a deploy (a manual `next build` without the full `pnpm build` script, or a deploy pipeline that only invokes `next build` directly), a `@google/genai` major bump installed via the `^2.21.0` caret range in package.json would ship to production with no runtime check to catch it — `generateStructured` would just start calling the new major's API shape unchecked.
- **Impact:** Lower likelihood than the other findings (CI's `build` job does run `pnpm build`, and `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` makes the installed version deterministic from the committed lockfile), but the code's own documentation claims a runtime assertion exists ("is asserted") when in practice all real request paths silently skip it. This is worth tightening since a version-drift surprise is explicitly the failure the pin exists to prevent, and today only a single, easily-bypassed build script stands between a mismatch and production.
- **Proposed fix:** Either call `assertPinnedSdk()` once at module load in lib/ai/adapter.ts (or lazily on the first `generateStructured` call) so a mismatched major fails fast at runtime too, or correct the adapter's doc comment to say the pin is enforced only at build time via scripts/verify-ai-runtime-trace.mjs, not asserted per-call.
- **Test gap:** lib/ai/adapter.test.ts tests assertPinnedSdk() as a pure function but has no test asserting generateStructured() calls it (or refuses to run on a mismatched major).
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The finding's factual claims all check out: assertPinnedSdk is defined at lib/ai/adapter.ts:37-44, its only callers are scripts/ai-canary.ts:77 and lib/ai/adapter.test.ts, and generateStructured (lib/ai/adapter.ts:288-32 | reproduction confirmed (high): Every factual claim in the finding checks out against the actual code, and the gap is reproducible by static inspection. ### 63. [medium] job_runs 'running' lock has no reaper — a crashed weekly-proposal run blocks all retries for that week

- **Location:** `app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts:60`
- **Evidence:** `takeLock` (app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts:60-68, reused by app/api/jobs/weekly-proposal/route.ts:23) inserts a `job_runs` row with `status: 'running'` and treats a unique-constraint violation (23505) as 'already locked'. `releaseLock` (lines 70-77) is only ever invoked from the handler's own try/catch (app/api/jobs/weekly-proposal/route.ts:76-89) — there is no TTL, no reaper, and no other code path anywhere in the repo (`grep -rn job_runs`) that ever flips a stuck 'running' row back to a retryable state.

The `job_runs` primary key (job, user_id, run_key) permanently blocks every later invocation for that same week — including a deliberate manual retry — because `takeLock` always sees the existing 'running' row and returns false, incrementing `out.skipped` with no error surfaced.

- **Test gap:** none found — no test simulates a crash between takeLock and releaseLock for weekly-proposal or estimate.
