### 64. [medium] Offline scan-cache always downgrades a blocked verdict to a generic 'unverified' warning

- **Location:** `lib/barcode/client.ts:116`
- **Evidence:** export function offlineMatch(cached: CachedRevision): BarcodeMatch | null {
  if (!cached.food_ref_id) return null;
  return {
    ...
    ingredientsText: null,
    allergenTags: [],
    attribution: cached.version_label,
    policy: { verdict: "unverified" },
  };
}
And components/BarcodeScanSheet.tsx:48-54 calls rememberScan(...) unconditionally for every 'resolved' response, regardless of the server-computed policy.verdict (blocked/unverified/allowed) -- but toCachedRevision (lib/barcode/client.ts:90-108) never stores allergenTags, ingredientsText, or the policy verdict itself in CachedRevision, only the raw nutrition/portion data.
- **Scenario:** Sasha scans a product online; the server correctly returns verdict: 'blocked', reason: 'allergen:gluten', and the item is cached via rememberScan for offline reuse. Later, offline (e.g. in a store with no signal), she rescans the same barcode. scanGtin throws ScanUnavailable, BarcodeScanSheet falls back to resolveOffline + offlineMatch (lib/barcode/client.ts:66-69), which unconditionally returns policy: { verdict: 'unverified' } -- the specific 'Contains gluten' alert badge she saw online is silently replaced by the generic amber 'No ingredient list -- check the pack' message, discarding the fact the product was already known-bad.
- **Impact:** The offline path is specifically meant to help her when the signal path guards work already (a repeat scan resolving from the phone), but it loses the strongest safety signal (an explicit blocked/'Contains X' alert) exactly in the scenario it's supposed to serve -- a rescan of a product she may already know is off-plan. Since the app's own design ('marking, never hiding') never blocks logging either way, the badge text is the only signal she gets, and this degrades it.
- **Proposed fix:** Persist enough of the online BarcodeMatch (at minimum allergenTags and the policy verdict/reason, not just nutrition data) into CachedRevision so offlineMatch can reconstruct the real verdict instead of always returning 'unverified'. At minimum, recompute evaluatePolicy client-side from the cached allergenTags against her last-synced constraint profile rather than discarding the information entirely.
- **Test gap:** tests/barcode/scan-cache.test.ts and tests/barcode/client.test.ts were not inspected for this specific offline-verdict-preservation case; there is no test asserting that a cached 'blocked' match still shows as blocked offline (the offlineMatch implementation hardcodes 'unverified' unconditionally, so such a test would currently fail).
- **Verify votes:** correctness refuted (high): The technical description is accurate — offlineMatch() at lib/barcode/client.ts:116-132 does hardcode policy:{verdict:"unverified"} regardless of the cached item's original server-computed verdict, because CachedRevision | reproduction confirmed (high): Fully reproduces from static analysis and a passing unit test that encodes this exact scenario. offlineMatch (lib/barcode/client.ts:116-133) hardcodes policy to {verdict:"unverified"}, allergenTags:[], ingredientsText:nu | impact confirmed (high): Confirmed by direct code read, not just by the finding's own quote. lib/barcode/client.ts:90-108 (toCachedRevision) writes only nutrition/portion fields into CachedRevision — no allergenTags, ingredientsText, or policy v

### 65. [medium] kind=weight export still paginates by offset with no tie-breaker column

- **Location:** `app/api/export/route.ts:189`
- **Evidence:** let query = client.from(source.from).select(source.select).order(source.order, { ascending: true }).range(from, from + PAGE_SIZE); — for kind=weight, source.order is "start_time" alone (SOURCES.weight, line 52), with no secondary unique key. This is the one live kind still on the pre-Phase-2 offset scheme (kind=meals in the same SOURCES map is dead code in practice, since `STREAMED.has("meals")` is true and the streamed branch returns first at line 174, before SOURCES is ever consulted for it). Every migrated kind explicitly moved away from this: lib/server/export.ts:6-9 states 'Paging is keyset over (local_date, eaten_at, id), not offset, so a 12,000-row log exports with no gap and no duplicate.' No test exercises kind=weight with more than zero rows (tests/export/streams.test.ts:139-149 only asserts the empty case).
- **Scenario:** If two weigh-ins ever land on the exact same `start_time` (duplicate import, clock-quantized entries, or simply enough history to cross a 1000-row page boundary at a tie), Postgres's ORDER BY on a non-unique column gives no guaranteed stable order across two separate `.range()` calls, so a row at the boundary can be duplicated or dropped between page N and page N+1.
- **Impact:** Low likelihood (weight is logged at most a few times a day and rarely at identical timestamps) but the failure mode — a silently skipped or duplicated weigh-in row in an exported/backed-up dataset — is exactly the class of bug the newer keyset design was built to eliminate everywhere else.
- **Proposed fix:** Add a unique tie-breaker (e.g. `id`) to the ORDER BY for the weight kind, or fold kind=weight into the same keyset-cursor pattern the other export kinds already use.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The finding holds up on re-read. ### 69. [medium] Bundled-catalog search misses plain plural queries like "eggs"

- **Location:** `lib/food-db.ts:103`
- **Evidence:** scoreName() only awards points when a query token is an exact word match, a prefix of a DB word, a substring of the joined name, or a subsequence of a DB word (which by construction requires the query to be no longer than the word it matches against) — a query token longer than every candidate word can never score. Confirmed by running the exact scoreName/tokens/subsequence code against the real lib/food-db.json rows: scoreName(tokens("eggs"), "Egg, whole, large") === 0, scoreName(tokens("eggs"), "Egg, hard-boiled") === 0, scoreName(tokens("eggs"), "Egg white") === 0, while scoreName(tokens("egg"), ...) scores 985-991. All ten USDA egg rows in food-db.json are named singular ("Egg, whole, large", "Egg white", "Egg roll", etc.) with no row containing the substring "eggs".
- **Scenario:** Offline (or with the catalog unavailable), she types the natural plural "eggs" into the search box — the same word her own preset food is literally named ("Eggs, large", lib/foods.ts:74, which IS found because it is stored as 'eggs' verbatim) — but if that food is not already in her `mine` list, or she is looking for a specific USDA prep like "Egg, hard-boiled", the entire bundled USDA egg category returns zero hits, with CatalogSearch.tsx showing "Nothing matches. Try fewer words, or type it from the label below."
- **Impact:** This is a general class of miss (any USDA/bundled row whose word is singular while she types the natural plural) affecting one of the most commonly logged proteins in her plan, and it is silent — she gets no hits and no indication that the food exists under a different form of the word, so she is nudged toward the manual "type it from the label" flow for a food that's already curated in the database.
- **Proposed fix:** Add a simple stem/plural-strip step before matching (e.g. try the query with a trailing 's' or 'es' removed as an additional candidate token) or fall back to a shorter subsequence check when the query is a simple plural of a DB word.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The core code claim is verified exactly as stated: scoreName() in lib/food-db.ts requires every query token to land as an exact word, a prefix, a substring of the joined name, or a subsequence of a DB word — a query toke | reproduction confirmed (high): The finding reproduces exactly as claimed. ### 71. [medium] Provider circuit breaker is per-instance in-memory state on a serverless deployment

- **Location:** `lib/ai/circuit.ts:10`
- **Evidence:** `const failures = new Map<string, number[]>();` (lib/ai/circuit.ts:10) is module-level, in-process state; the file's own comment says so explicitly: "Module-level, per instance: `failures` maps provider → failure timestamps... five failures in ten minutes open the circuit." `assertCircuitClosed`/`recordFailure` only ever read/write this in-memory map — there is no database or KV-backed counter. app/api/coach/route.ts and the other AI-calling routes run with `export const runtime = "nodejs"` on Vercel (per AGENTS.md, Vercel Git integration builds production from main), where Next.js API routes execute as independent serverless function instances that can cold-start on any request.
- **Scenario:** Gemini has a bad stretch (the kind docs/ai-data-handling.md itself records happening on 2026-09-13, where 3.8 and 3.7 exhausted retries with 503 before the pin moved to 3.6). Each of Sasha's coach turns or capture calls that lands on a fresh/cold serverless instance starts `recentFailureCount('google')` back at 0, so the 5-failures-in-10-minutes threshold in `assertCircuitClosed` (circuit.ts:25-33) may never actually accumulate across instances even though the provider is genuinely failing repeatedly for her.
- **Impact:** The circuit breaker's purpose — stop hammering (and paying for retries against) a sick provider once it's shown a failure pattern — can silently not engage in production, because the failure count it depends on resets on every cold start instead of accumulating per-user/per-deployment the way the daily spend cap and turn budget were deliberately redesigned to do (assertTurnBudget in lib/server/coach.ts:71-73 explicitly notes "a serverless cold start forgets a Map" as the reason it counts from `ai_calls` instead — the same reasoning was not applied to the circuit breaker).
- **Proposed fix:** Back the circuit breaker's failure counter with the same durable store used for the spend cap and turn budget (a recent-failures query against `ai_calls`, or a small dedicated table/KV entry), the same way lib/server/coach.ts already does for `assertTurnBudget`, instead of a module-level Map that a serverless cold start clears.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Every factual claim in the finding checks out against the actual code. lib/ai/circuit.ts:10 is a bare module-level `const failures = new Map<string, number[]>()`; assertCircuitClosed (lines 25-33) and recordFailure (line | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduces and is accurately evidenced. lib/ai/circuit.ts:10 (`const failures = new Map<string, number[]>();`) is confirmed module-level, in-process state — `assertCircuitClosed`/`recordFailure` (circuit.ts:25-39) never  | impact refuted (high): The technical claim is accurate (lib/ai/circuit.ts:10, confirmed by its own comment) but the impact claim does not hold up. Two things neutralize it for Sasha specifically:

1. The circuit breaker is purely a fail-fast/c

### 73. [medium] Voice capture has no keyboard path and conflates permission-denied with unsupported device

- **Location:** `components/VoiceCapture.tsx:155`
- **Evidence:** RecordButton only wires onPointerDown/onPointerUp/onPointerCancel/onPointerLeave (lines 58-63) — no onKeyDown/onClick handler starts or stops a recording, so a native button activation (Enter/Space, or many assistive-technology 'activate' actions, which dispatch click/keydown) never begins recording. Separately, beginRecording's single catch block (lines 155-158) sets phase="unsupported" for BOTH pickAudioMimeType returning null (device/browser lacks any supported MIME type) and any getUserMedia rejection (including the user simply denying the mic permission prompt), and both render the identical copy in UnsupportedNotice: 'This phone will not record here. Type it instead, or use search.'
- **Scenario:** 1) A keyboard or switch-control user tabs to the record button and activates it — nothing happens, since activation dispatches a click, not the pointerdown+pointerup pair beginRecording listens for; there is no way to record voice without a literal touch-and-hold gesture. 2) Sasha taps 'Don't Allow' on the mic permission prompt, or had previously denied it in Settings — she is told 'This phone will not record here,' which reads as a device limitation rather than 'grant microphone access,' and has no path to discover the fix.
- **Impact:** Voice capture (one of the app's meal-logging entry points) is unreachable by any non-touch input, and a simple, reversible permission denial is presented as a permanent device incapability with no corrective guidance.
- **Proposed fix:** Add onClick/onKeyDown (Enter/Space) handling so the control is keyboard-operable. Split the catch in beginRecording: on NotAllowedError show 'Microphone access is off. Allow it in Settings, then try again.' and keep the generic unsupported message only for a true pickAudioMimeType failure or NotFoundError.
- **Test gap:** none

### 74. [medium] Lint CI step cannot fail — eslint-config-next rules are warn-level, no --max-warnings

- **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:30`
- **Evidence:** ci.yml:30 runs `pnpm lint`, and package.json:9 defines `"lint": "eslint"` with no `--max-warnings` flag. eslint.config.mjs (repo root) is just `[...nextVitals, ...nextTs]` plus a globalIgnores block — it adds no custom `rules` section, so every rule keeps eslint-config-next's own severity. Running `pnpm lint` on HEAD today (verified in the scratchpad) prints `✖ 11 problems (0 errors, 11 warnings)` and exits 0 — e.g. app/api/constraints/route.ts:3 `'HttpError' is defined but never used`, and lib/health-client.ts:86 `Do not use window.location.assign() ... @next/next/no-location-assign-relative-destination` (a real full-page-reload bug risk for a PWA). Both are only warnings, so CI is green.
- **Scenario:** A PR introduces any eslint-config-next warning-level issue — an unused import, a missing hook dependency (react-hooks/exhaustive-deps is warn in this preset), or another window.location.assign() instead of the router — anywhere in the diff. `eslint` exits 0 because it has zero *errors*, `pnpm lint` exits 0, the Lint step is green, and the PR merges with the regression unreviewed.
- **Impact:** For Sasha's one-person PWA, this specific rule (no-location-assign) flags exactly the kind of bug that silently breaks the app-shell/offline experience (a hard navigation instead of a client-side route change can drop in-flight sync/outbox state); nothing in CI can ever catch it or its siblings because the lint gate has no enforceable threshold.
- **Proposed fix:** Add `--max-warnings 0` to the lint script (`"lint": "eslint --max-warnings 0"`), or promote the rules that matter (no-location-assign-relative-destination, no-unused-vars, react-hooks/exhaustive-deps) to "error" in eslint.config.mjs. Then fix (or explicitly allow) the 11 current warnings so the tightened gate starts green.
- **Test gap:** none
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The core technical claim is verified true by direct reproduction, though two of the finding's line citations are off. package.json's "lint" script is `"eslint"` (actual line 10, not 9 — line 9 is `"postinstall": ...`); c | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduced exactly. `pnpm lint` on HEAD (3ca35cb) exits 0 while printing "11 problems (0 errors, 11 warnings)" — confirmed with a direct run capturing the real process exit code (ACTUAL_EXIT=0), not just visual output. p | impact refuted (high): The technical claim is accurate but the impact chain does not hold up. Verified: package.json:10 (not :9) defines "lint": "eslint" with no --max-warnings, ci.yml's Lint step is at line 45 (not :30) running `pnpm lint`, e

### 80. [medium] meals.test.ts dairy/gluten scan is narrower than bannedReason for ghee/whey/casein/couscous

Compare lib/foods.ts:147-148, the manual-entry guard used when Sasha types a food herself: `if (/\bmilk\b|cheese|yogurt|yoghurt|\bbutter\b|\bghee\b|whey|casein|cream\b|ice cream/.test(n)) return "That is dairy...";` and line 145: `if (/wheat|\bbread\b|pasta|barley|\brye\b|couscous|seitan|flour tortilla/.test(n)) return "That has gluten...";`. `ghee`, `whey`, `casein` (dairy) and `couscous` (gluten) are all explicitly banned by `bannedReason` but are absent from the meals.test.ts word lists that police the hardcoded rotation library.
- **Scenario:** A future edit to lib/meals.ts adds a fat option named 'Ghee' (a common paleo/keto fat swap) to a rotation slot, or a carb option named 'Couscous salad'. `no component name contains a gluten or dairy term` in lib/meals.test.ts (the guard for exactly this file) passes cleanly because 'ghee' and 'couscous' are not in its local word lists — even though the identical string typed into the app by Sasha herself would be refused outright by `bannedReason` in lib/foods.ts. The safety net that is supposed to catch this class of mistake in the curated meal library is strictly weaker than the guard on her own manual typing, for the same words.
- **Impact:** Sasha has ulcerative colitis with dairy and gluten as confirmed triggers. The hardcoded meal/rotation library (lib/meals.ts) is the primary source of what she is told to eat — unlike a typed entry, she has no `bannedReason` check in front of it, only this test. A dairy or gluten item slipping into the rotation library would be served to her on a live date with CI green, with no independent runtime guard to catch it (the rotation path does not call bannedReason).

### 81. [medium] Oura OAuth callback rate limit uses one global key

Each one increments the same single counter before the state/code check runs, so the 21st request from anyone — including Sasha's own browser completing the real Oura consent redirect — gets a 429 and is bounced back to `/settings?oura=error&reason=rate_limited` for up to the rest of that hour.
- **Impact:** The one feature that keeps her Oura ring's sleep/readiness/activity data flowing (reconnecting after a token issue, or the very first Connect Oura) can be denied by anyone hitting a predictable public URL a handful of times, with Sasha seeing only a generic rate-limited error and no way to know it was caused by unrelated traffic rather than her own actions.
- **Proposed fix:** Scope the callback's rate-limit key to something the caller can't spoof for free — e.g. per `state` value (which is only known to whoever Oura's real redirect carries it to) after it's read, or per source IP as a coarse fallback, rather than one process-wide bucket shared by every caller.

### 85. [medium] Progress-export row cap assumes an unenforced 20-codes-per-date bound

- **Location:** `lib/server/export.ts:298`
- **Evidence:** streamProgress computes `const cap = (opts.limit + 1) * NUTRIENT_CODES.length;` (line 298) and readProgressRows fetches cap+1 rows (lines 220-232), relying on groupTotalsByDate's "drop the last (possibly partial) date group" logic (lines 244-262). `hasMore = dates.length > opts.limit` (line 304) is computed purely from how many complete dates fit inside that capped read, not from whether more data exists beyond the last returned date. The bound of "at most 20 rows per date" comes only from daily_totals never emitting a code absent from catalog_nutrients (20260918090200_phase2_daily_totals.sql:4-7, jsonb_each(li.snapshot)), and catalog_nutrients is explicitly "filled by a worker, not a migration" (20260918090300_phase2_catalog_dri_seed.sql:1-3) — disconnected from the hand-maintained NUTRIENT_CODES literal (lib/nutrients/codes.ts:7-11) by nothing but convention. tests/export/progress-paging.test.ts only ever constructs rows with exactly one nutrient code per date (lines 10-13), so the real 20-codes-per-date shape this cap math is built for has zero test coverage.
- **Scenario:** A future catalog import adds a 21st tracked nutrient code to catalog_nutrients without someone also updating NUTRIENT_CODES in lib/nutrients/codes.ts. Once a capped read spans a run of dates carrying 21 daily_totals rows, readProgressRows returns fewer complete dates than opts.limit even though more data exists past the last one — hasMore reads false, next_cursor comes back null, and a "download all my progress" export stops early with no error, silently, on both the JSON and CSV paths.
- **Impact:** A plausible, low-visibility future data change (adding a nutrient the app already claims to track) would silently truncate her nutrition-progress export with no signal, and the one existing test for this code path can't catch it because it never uses more than one code per date.
- **Proposed fix:** Make hasMore/next_cursor depend on whether the raw read actually hit the cap+1 row ceiling (rows.length > cap) rather than on dates.length > opts.limit, decoupling truncation detection from the per-date row-count assumption; add a test using more than NUTRIENT_CODES.length rows for a single date.
- **Test gap:** tests/export/progress-paging.test.ts only tests groupTotalsByDate with exactly one row per date; it never tests the interaction between per-date row count and NUTRIENT_CODES.length that the production cap formula depends on.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Re-read lib/server/export.ts:218-262 and 294-309 line-for-line; the cited lines match exactly (cap formula at 298, readProgressRows fetching cap+1 rows at 220-232, groupTotalsByDate's drop-partial-last-date logic at 244- | reproduction confirmed (high): All cited evidence checks out verbatim, and I reproduced the actual failure by importing the real, unmodified `groupTotalsByDate` (and `NUTRIENT_CODES`) from the repo via `node --conditions=react-server --import tsx` (th | impact confirmed (medium): Verified the full mechanism end to end and the technical claim holds. lib/server/export.ts:298 computes `cap = (opts.limit + 1) * NUTRIENT_CODES.length`; readProgressRows (export.ts ~L220-232) fetches cap+1 rows ordered

### 90. [medium] "no gaps" test in rotation.test.ts is tautological, can't catch a missing slot

- **Location:** `lib/rotation.test.ts:116`
- **Evidence:** it("returns slots in eating order with no gaps", () => {
  const date = "2026-09-09";
  const day = mealsForDay(date);
  const slots = day.map((d) => d.slot);
  expect(slots).toEqual(slotListFor("training-7-fasted").filter((s: SlotId) => slots.includes(s)));
  expect(day.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
});
The expected array is built by filtering the canonical slot list down to `slots.includes(s)` — i.e. down to exactly the slots that are ALREADY present in the actual result. For any `slot` missing from `day` (pool empty for that slot on that date), `slots.includes(slot)` is false, so the filter drops it from the expected array too. LHS and RHS are then identical by construction regardless of what got dropped: this assertion cannot fail on a missing slot, only on a reordering of the slots that did survive.
- **Scenario:** Any regression that empties a slot's pool for a given day type/date — the exact class of bug already found at lib/foods.ts:333 (a whole macro role marked Avoid drops a slot), or any future one in poolFor/rotationPool/composeMeal — produces an incomplete `mealsForDay` output. This test, and the neighbouring "produces a complete day for an arbitrary far-future date — c12" test (line 124), only assert `day.length > 0` and that present meals are truthy; neither asserts that every slot in `slotListFor(dayType)` for that day type is actually present. Both pass unchanged on an incomplete day.
- **Impact:** AGENTS.md's guarded rule #4 — "Any date renders a complete day; there is no start date and no end date" — and rotation.ts's own docstring promise ("Everything is a pure function of the calendar date... the same date always yields the same meal") have no test that would fail if a slot silently vanished. For Sasha (UC, phone-first, one person relying on this app to tell her what to eat) a day rendering with a missing meal slot — e.g. no fat option after an Avoid edit — ships and stays shipped with CI green, because the one test whose name claims to guard exactly this can never fail on it.
- **Proposed fix:** Replace the self-referential filter with an independent completeness check, e.g. `expect(slots).toEqual(slotListFor(dayType))` (asserting every canonical slot for that day type is present, in canonical order) instead of filtering the canonical list by the already-computed `slots`. ### 91. [medium] Dedupe key has no type disambiguation when a source supplies its own vendor_sample_id

- **Location:** `lib/health/dedupe.ts:12`
- **Evidence:** export function dedupeKey(s: { vendor_sample_id?: string; type: string;
                               start_time: string; end_time: string }): string {
  if (typeof s.vendor_sample_id === "string" && s.vendor_sample_id.length > 0) {
    return s.vendor_sample_id;
  }
  return `${s.type}|${epochSeconds(s.start_time)}|${epochSeconds(s.end_time)}`;
}

-- mirrored exactly in the DB generated column --
supabase/migrations/20260911000400_privileged.sql:26-28:
  dedupe_key text generated always as (coalesce(vendor_sample_id,
    type || '|' || ...)) stored);
create unique index health_samples_dedupe on health_samples (user_id, source_id, dedupe_key);

-- the UPDATE branch that fires on a dedupe collision never touches type --
supabase/migrations/20260925000000_phase5_health_ingest.sql:109-112:
    on conflict (user_id, source_id, dedupe_key) do update
       set value = excluded.value, unit = excluded.unit, end_time = excluded.end_time,
           device_label = excluded.device_label, ingest_event_id = excluded.ingest_event_id,
           vendor_updated_at = excluded.vendor_updated_at
- **Scenario:** When vendor_sample_id is present the effective identity of a health observation is the vendor id ALONE - type is never folded in. Only lib/oura/map.ts is careful to defend against this, by hand-crafting vendor_sample_id: `${id}:${type}` (map.ts:19) so its own samples never collide across metric types. The Shortcut ingest path (lib/health/normalize.ts:117-119, normalizeShortcut) passes through whatever vendor_sample_id the phone-side Shortcut script supplies, completely unchecked and undocumented as needing a type suffix. If that script (or any future integration reusing this same field) ever emits the same vendor_sample_id for two different sample types from the same source (e.g. sleep_asleep and sleep_in_bed derived from one exported record), the second insert collides on the unique index (user_id, source_id, dedupe_key) and lands in the on conflict do update branch, which overwrites value/unit/end_time but never updates type - so the second sample's true type is silently discarded and its numeric value is stored/labeled under the FIRST sample's type instead.
- **Impact:** A silent cross-metric data corruption with no rejected/error signal anywhere in the response (rejected stays empty, the row is reported as updated) - e.g. a sleep-in-bed reading could silently overwrite what's stored as sleep_asleep (or vice versa), corrupting the number Sasha sees for her own sleep on the Body dashboard, with nothing in the ingest response or logs indicating anything went wrong.
- **Proposed fix:** Fold type into the dedupe key unconditionally, even when vendor_sample_id is present, e.g. dedupe_key = type || ':' || coalesce(vendor_sample_id, start|end) in both the TS dedupeKey() and the DB generated column, so two different metric types can never collide regardless of what a source's vendor id looks like.
- **Test gap:** lib/health/dedupe.test.ts (18-line source) - no test supplies two different types sharing one vendor_sample_id to confirm they don't collide.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The mechanism described is real and verified: lib/health/dedupe.ts:12-16 and the generated column in supabase/migrations/20260911000400_privileged.sql:26-28 both coalesce on vendor_sample_id ALONE, ignoring type; the ON  | reproduction confirmed (high): The finding's code-level mechanism is 100% accurate and I reproduced the JS half directly (dedupe key collision) and confirmed the DB half by reading the exact SQL (generated column, unique index, and ON CONFLICT SET lis | impact refuted (high): The technical mechanism is real: dedupeKey() (lib/health/dedupe.ts:12-17) and the DB generated column (supabase/migrations/20260911000400_privileged.sql:26-28) both use vendor_sample_id alone as the identity when present

  weight_body_mass: "body_mass", resting_heart_rate: "resting_heart_rate",
  heart_rate_variability: "hrv_sdnn", sleep_analysis: "sleep_asleep",

-- versus the documented invariant it violates --

  "hrv_sdnn", "sleep_asleep", "sleep_in_bed"] as const;
- **Scenario:** INGEST_SAMPLE_TYPES (used to validate the Shortcut ingest path's zod schema at contract.ts:29) deliberately excludes basal_energy - the code comment states basal energy is Oura-only. - **Impact:** Currently basal_energy is written but never read anywhere downstream (rollUpDay in lib/health/daily.ts has no branch for it, and no other file in the repo references basal_energy outside contract.ts/normalize.ts/units.ts), so today it's silent dead data rather than a visible wrong number. But it is a live contract violation: the moment any future TDEE/expenditure work starts trusting basal_energy as Oura-quality resting-metabolism data - exactly the assumption the contract.ts comment documents - it will silently be polluted by phone/Watch estimates that already flowed in from HAE, with no schema or code path currently preventing or even flagging it.
- **Proposed fix:** Either drop basal_energy_burned from HAE_METRIC_TYPES (reject it explicitly, matching the documented Oura-only invariant), or if phone-sourced basal energy is actually intended to be accepted, update contract.ts's comment and INGEST_SAMPLE_TYPES to say so and make normalizeHealthAutoExport's output pass through the same INGEST_SAMPLE_TYPES-scoped validation the Shortcut path already has, so drift between the two ingest paths can't reintroduce this silently again.
- **Test gap:** docs/specs/phase-5-body-data.md:291 already notes the Phase 5 pipeline ships none of its specified unit tests (a previously-reported finding); there is specifically no test asserting normalizeHealthAutoExport rejects or excludes basal_energy_burned the way the Shortcut schema's INGEST_SAMPLE_TYPES enum does.

A tsx repro against the live repo files -- feeding {name:"basal_ener | impact refuted (high): The technical claim is verified and accurate: lib/health/normalize.ts:12 maps HAE's basal_energy_burned to type "basal_energy", and lib/server/health-ingest.ts's HAE branch (parsePayload, ~lines 55-67) calls normalizeHea

### 94. [low] AI spend cap and capture daily cap are check-then-act, not atomic

- **Location:** `lib/ai/adapter.ts:82`
- **Evidence:** `assertUnderSpendCap` (lines 82-103) reads the sum of today's `cost_estimate` from `ai_calls` and throws only if `spent >= cap`; the provider call and the row that adds to `spent` both happen strictly after this read, inside `generateStructured` (line 299 onward). The equivalent capture-side check, `assertUnderCaps` in lib/server/capture.ts (lines 130-152), has the same shape: read `ai_calls_today`, compare to `hardCap()`, then proceed. Neither takes a lock or uses a conditional/atomic increment.
- **Scenario:** Two concurrent requests (Sasha double-taps a capture button, or has two tabs open) that both start before either has written its `ai_calls` row will both read the same `spent`/`usedToday` value, both see themselves under the cap, and both proceed — so the day's spend or capture count can exceed `AI_DAILY_SPEND_CAP_USD` / `CAPTURE_DAILY_HARD_CAP` by the number of concurrent in-flight requests.
- **Impact:** Low real-world exposure for a single-user phone app (true concurrency is rare), and lib/ai/spend.ts's own comment frames the cap as "a brake on a runaway loop, not an invoice," so a small overshoot is within the design's stated tolerance. Still, this is the "spend caps that can be bypassed" class the audit asked about, and the overshoot is unbounded in principle under enough concurrent requests (e.g. a client-side retry storm during a provider outage, which is also when spend is likeliest to run away).
- **Proposed fix:** If tighter enforcement is wanted, move the cap check into the same write as the `ai_calls` insert (e.g. a Postgres function that atomically re-sums and rejects under a transaction/advisory lock) rather than a separate read-then-call. Given the current framing as a soft brake, this may be an accepted tradeoff worth documenting explicitly rather than fixing.
- **Test gap:** No test exercises two concurrent generateStructured/proposeDraft calls racing past the same cap check.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): The finding is accurate. Verified by direct reading: lib/ai/adapter.ts:289-334 (generateStructured) calls assertUnderSpendCap (lines 82-103, a plain SELECT+client-side sum of cost_estimate with no lock) at line 299, then | reproduction confirmed (high): Reproduced directly against the real code. lib/ai/adapter.ts:82-103 (`assertUnderSpendCap`) sums `cost_estimate` via `.select().eq().gte()` and only throws if `spent >= cap`; the provider call and the `ai_calls` insert b | impact refuted (high): The technical claim checks out, but it fails the impact bar this audit is judging against. The failure mode is a possible small overshoot of an internal AI-spend/capture-count guard under true concurrency (double-tap, tw

### 96. [low] duplicate_groups upsert only guards the id primary key, not the (user_id, fingerprint) unique constraint two devices can race

- **Location:** `supabase/migrations/20260919090300_phase3_sync_entities.sql:269`
- **Evidence:** `create table duplicate_groups (... unique (user_id, fingerprint))` (supabase/migrations/20260911000300_sync.sql:23-27). `_sync_upsert`'s `duplicate_groups` branch (supabase/migrations/20260919090300_phase3_sync_entities.sql:269-281) only handles a collision `on conflict (id)` — there is no `on conflict (user_id, fingerprint) do nothing`/merge path. Every other tombstone-capable entity here has a client-generated random-UUID `id`, so two devices independently computing the identical fingerprint (lib/sync/fingerprint.ts is a pure function of date/slot/time-bucket/items, so two devices WILL compute the same hash for the same real-world duplicate) will each mint a different `id` for the `create` op, and the second device's insert raises an unhandled unique-constraint violation instead of being recognized as the same logical group.
- **Scenario:** Sasha logs the same meal twice close together on two devices while both are briefly offline (e.g. phone and a second phone/tablet). Each device's client-side dedupe logic detects the same possible-duplicate pair and independently queues a `duplicate_groups` `create` op with the same fingerprint but a different random `id`. The first device's create op applies fine (rev 1). The second device's create hits the `unique (user_id, fingerprint)` constraint inside `_sync_upsert`, which is not caught anywhere in `apply_sync_op`, so the RPC call raises, `app/api/sync/ops/route.ts`'s per-op try/catch turns it into `{status:'error', message: ...}`, and the client's `parkOrRetry` (lib/sync/outbox.ts:175-185) retries the identical op up to MAX_ERROR_ATTEMPTS (3) and then parks it permanently — it will fail identically forever since the underlying uniqueness conflict never resolves on retry.
- **Impact:** Currently unreachable in the shipped app — no client code creates `duplicate_groups` ops yet (only lib/sync/keys.ts, lib/sync/merge.ts, lib/sync/types.ts and tests reference the entity), so this cannot bite Sasha today. It is a latent defect in already-deployed, already-fully-wired sync plumbing (SQL upsert, client merge/key encoding) that will silently and permanently strand one device's duplicate-group op — with no user-facing signal beyond an inert 'Sync now' retry that keeps failing — the moment the dedupe-card feature (AGENTS.md Phase 1/2 duplicate-group UI) starts producing these ops from two devices.
- **Proposed fix:** Add `on conflict (user_id, fingerprint) do update set ... where duplicate_groups.user_id = p_user_id` (or a pre-check that resolves the existing group by fingerprint and reuses its id) to the `duplicate_groups` branch of `_sync_upsert`, so two devices detecting the same duplicate converge on one group instead of racing a raw constraint violation.
- **Test gap:** No test in lib/sync/merge.test.ts, lib/sync/outbox.test.ts, or tests/rls exercises two concurrent `duplicate_groups` creates with the same fingerprint.
- **Verify votes:** correctness confirmed (high): Every link in the claimed causal chain checks out against the current, effective SQL and TypeScript. ### 97. [low] Signed media upload URL is valid far longer than the 60s the app promises

- **Location:** `lib/server/media.ts:18`
- **Evidence:** export const SIGNED_UPLOAD_SECONDS = 60; ... (line 58) const signed = await admin.storage.from(MEDIA_BUCKET).createSignedUploadUrl(objectKey(path)); ... (line 67) return { path, uploadUrl: signed.data.signedUrl, expiresInSeconds: SIGNED_UPLOAD_SECONDS };  -- verified against the installed SDK (node_modules/.pnpm/@supabase+storage-js@2.116.0/.../umd/supabase.js): `createSignedUploadUrl(e,n)` takes only a path and upsert option and calls POST `${url}/object/upload/sign/${path}` with no expiry parameter at all -- there is no code path that could make the returned token expire in 60 seconds. Supabase Storage's signed-upload tokens are minted server-side with a fixed multi-hour lifetime (not configurable through this client call), so SIGNED_UPLOAD_SECONDS is never communicated to Storage and is purely a label the client is told to trust.
- **Scenario:** Sasha's phone calls POST /api/media/sign for a photo capture. The server hands back { uploadUrl, expiresInSeconds: 60 }. The client (or any component/log/proxy that captures that URL -- e.g. a browser history entry, a crash report, a debugging proxy, or a retried request queued after a network drop) can still successfully PUT a file to that exact uploadUrl long after 60 seconds have passed -- the real token Storage issued remains valid for its own multi-hour default window. Nothing else in the sign/upload path (no separate short-lived DB check, no re-validation of elapsed time) enforces the 60s figure.
- **Impact:** The security model documented in this file's own header comment ("a foreign path costs one string comparison and zero bytes of download") and the API contract (spec §5's '60/min... 30/min/user' framing, expiresInSeconds returned to the client) both assume a short-lived, single-use-ish upload window. In practice a captured or replayed uploadUrl remains a live write-capability into Sasha's private media bucket (under her own user_id prefix) for the full multi-hour Storage default -- roughly two orders of magnitude longer than intended. For a single-person health app this raises the blast radius of any URL leak (shared screenshot, synced browser history, proxy/log capture) well beyond what the code assumes or what its own tests are likely to check.
- **Proposed fix:** Either accept and document Storage's real signed-upload lifetime (drop the misleading expiresInSeconds:60 from the response, or replace it with the actual value Storage enforces), or add an explicit server-side short-lived check: record the sign time in media_objects and reject/ignore an upload whose object shows up after N seconds (the media-sweep job already visits media_objects and could tombstone/reject stale-but-uploaded objects), so the enforced window matches the one the code and its comments claim.
- **Test gap:** none

## 3. Coverage

Stopped after 4 finder rounds on user instruction; rounds were still producing fresh findings. No gap round was run.

Four rounds produced 98 confirmed + 38 refuted findings touching 94 distinct files out of 591 in-scope files — a normal footprint for a targeted defect hunt (findings cluster at bug sites, not at every file read), but three things point to real gaps rather than just selective citation: (1) whole named subsystems have zero footprint on either side of the ledger despite a matching finder dimension existing, (2) six locations show the same root cause re-surfacing across separate rounds (rounds 1/2/3 in one case) rather than being deduped, and (3) every confirmed scenario I sampled is a code-trace narrative ("Sasha types X, bannedReason returns null, it is then eligible...") with line citations, not an executed reproduction (no test output, no curl/HTTP transcript, no observed runtime behavior) — so "confirmed" here means "the code trace holds up to a second reader," not "someone hit the bug." That distinction matters most for findings whose truth depends on runtime/deployment behavior the reader can't settle by inspection alone (serverless instance lifecycle, CI runner env vars, browser SW caching, Supabase Storage signed-URL enforcement).

**Same root cause reported more than once:**
- #1, #9, #12: lib/foods.ts:148 bannedReason uses an enumerated word/regex match instead of a semantically complete banned-ingredient check, so any dairy/gluten term not on its exact list (or defeated by a word-boundary on a compound word) passes as allowed. Found independently in rounds 1, 2, and 3 with different example inputs (buttermilk/malt; unspecified 'plausible names'; named cheeses without the word 'cheese') — same function, same defect, never fixed between rounds so kept resurfacing.
- #75, #80: lib/meals.test.ts:50's dairy/gluten word list used to test bannedReason is itself a hand-maintained list that has drifted narrower than production's bannedReason — round 2 found it via the buttermilk/malt gap, round 3 via ghee/whey/casein/couscous. Same underlying defect (the test's word list, not the production list) reported twice.
- #32, #63: app/api/jobs/estimate/route.ts:60's job_runs lock acquisition has no failure-path release and no timeout/reaper, so any non-clean exit (crash) or even a clean thrown error leaves the lock held and blocks retries for the week. Rounds 1 and 2 each described one half of the same missing-release-path defect (crash vs clean failure) at the identical line.
- #59, #62: components/ProposalCard.tsx:37's approve/reject handlers have no error handling at all; #62 (silently swallows a stale-proposal 409) is a specific instance of the general defect #59 already names. Both anchor the same missing try/catch.

**Confirmed findings whose evidence was not reproduced (code-reading claims):**
- Nearly all 98 confirmed findings share this property, not just the ones below — I'm flagging the subset where the gap between code-reading and demonstrated fact is highest-stakes.
- #71 (circuit.ts per-instance in-memory breaker) — the scenario asserts cold-start behavior on 'a serverless deployment' from reading assertCircuitClosed/recentFailureCount; nobody actually deployed to Vercel and forced two cold instances to prove the counter resets. If the AI routes are configured with min-instances or a shared runtime, the claim could be weaker than stated.
- #18 and #52 (CI silently skips RLS/cross-user and dozens of other suites) — built on reading ci.yml's HAS_BRANCH-gated step, not on an actual CI run log showing the step was skipped on a real PR. A live Actions run (or Blacksmith log) was never pulled to confirm the gate fires the way the YAML implies in the runner's actual env.
- #74 (lint step can't fail on warn-level rules) — inferred from eslint-config-next's rule severities and the absence of --max-warnings in package.json's lint script; no one actually introduced a warn-level violation and ran pnpm lint to watch it exit 0.
- #16 (SW cache masks offline state on /body) — traced through public/sw.js's networkFirst fallback logic; not reproduced in an actual browser/PWA install with the network toggled off mid-session.
- #97 (signed media upload URL usable past 60s) — asserts Supabase Storage doesn't itself enforce the expiresInSeconds window narrowly; this is a claim about a third-party service's behavior, not something confirmed by actually PUTting to an expired signed URL.
- #87 (ai-canary never probes Coach's pinned model) — reads scripts/ai-canary.ts's target model list against lib/ai/models.ts's pin; plausible and probably correct, but no one ran pnpm ai:canary and diffed its output against the Coach route's actual runtime model to show the blind spot in practice.
- #93 (six down migrations have zero test coverage, 'nobody has ever run these') — a grep-for-references claim; no one actually executed supabase migration down against a branch to confirm the SQL is broken or merely untested.
- #60 (assertPinnedSdk never called outside tests/canary) — a call-graph claim from reading the function's call sites; not demonstrated by actually building without the guard and observing an unpinned SDK ship.
- #90 (rotation.test.ts 'no gaps' test is tautological) — argued by reading the assertion's logic; not demonstrated by actually mutating poolFor to empty a slot and showing the existing test still passes.
- #71/#87/#60 all reason from docs/ai-data-handling.md's own incident log (the 3.8/3.7 503 story) as corroborating evidence, which is itself a project narrative document, not independent reproduction.

**Unexplored areas a fifth round should cover:**
- **auth-security dimension produced no findings on the actual auth code** (lib/server/auth.ts, lib/supabase/client.ts + server.ts, app/auth/callback/route.ts, app/sign-in/{page,code,password}.ts, components/AuthGate.tsx, components/PasswordSignInForm.tsx, components/SignInCodeForm.tsx): Every 'security' finding that survived (#18, #52) is about CI skipping RLS tests, not about the auth implementation itself. None of the actual session/callback/sign-in-code code paths appear in confirmed or refuted — for a dimension explicitly named 'auth-security', the auth surface itself looks unread.
- **privileged/token/crypto boundary never touched** (lib/server/privileged.ts, lib/server/tokens.ts, lib/server/health-tokens.ts, lib/server/admin.ts, lib/server/crypto.ts, lib/server/ratelimit.ts (the implementation, not just its call sites), lib/server/http.ts): AGENTS.md calls this out by name as the service-role/privileged-access boundary and the encrypted-health-token layer — exactly the kind of code a security-minded finder round should read line by line. Zero findings (confirmed or refuted) anchor here despite two dimensions (auth-security, health-ingest-oura) that should have led a finder to it.
- **recipe / build-a-meal feature almost entirely unexamined** (components/BuildMealSheet.tsx, RecipeSheet.tsx, RecipeDraftList.tsx, RecipeFoodRow.tsx, RecipeFoodSearch.tsx, RecipeIngredientPicker.tsx, RecipeItemRow.tsx, lib/recipes/nutrients.ts, lib/recipes/recipe-sheet-state.ts, lib/foods/adopt-client.ts): This is a headline diet-safety feature per AGENTS.md ('Tap one protein, one carb, one fat -> portions sized to the slot') yet only lib/recipes/use-recipe-sheet.ts:238 (a generic 'save has no error handling' finding) touches this cluster. The actual portion-sizing and nutrient math a wrong recipe could put in front of Sasha was never traced.
- **body/health dashboard rendering layer disconnected from the health-ingest findings** (components/BodyDashboard.tsx, BodyDataCard.tsx, BodyProfileFields.tsx, BodyTodayCard.tsx/Gate.tsx/Live.tsx, HealthMetricCard.tsx, WeightTrend.tsx, Sparkline.tsx, lib/health/capability.ts, contract.ts, replay.ts, today-card.ts, units.ts): health-ingest-oura and expenditure-tdee-weekly-proposals as dimensions concentrated entirely on ingestion/estimation math (daily.ts, dedupe.ts, normalize.ts, oura/map.ts, expenditure.ts) and never followed corrupted/mismatched data into what Sasha actually sees on /body — only TdeeLines.tsx got one finding.
- **barcode decode/quorum core and raw catalog source mappers** (lib/barcode/quorum.ts, lib/barcode/gtin.ts, lib/barcode/scan-cache.ts, lib/barcode/worker.ts, lib/catalog/off.ts, lib/catalog/fdc.ts, lib/catalog/fdc-map.ts): barcode-scan-diet-safety reached lib/catalog/off-map.ts and lib/server/barcode.ts but never the OCR/decoder quorum-voting logic (worker.ts/quorum.ts) or the raw OFF/FDC source-to-catalog mappers that feed everything downstream — a wrong vote or a bad source mapping upstream would explain several already-confirmed downstream symptoms but was never traced to its origin.
- **coach orchestration core (turn/dispatch/policy) never read directly** (lib/coach/turn.ts, lib/coach/dispatch.ts, lib/coach/policy.ts, lib/coach/deflect.ts, lib/coach/registry.ts, lib/coach/prompt.ts, lib/coach/weekly-review.ts): coach-ai reached envelope.ts (prompt injection), executors.ts, and floors.ts, but the module that actually wires a turn through policy checks into a draft (turn.ts/dispatch.ts/policy.ts) was never opened — exactly where the already-found pattern of hardcoded category:null / foodRefId:null / discarded allergenTags (across executors.ts, capture.ts, log.ts, catalog.ts, all refuted-but-real) would most plausibly trace to one shared root cause.

## 4. Suggested fix order

All 98 confirmed findings are covered exactly once across 70 branches, ordered by worst-finding severity (critical → high → medium → low) and then, within each severity tier, by blast radius using this rank order: sync/data-loss and core-rules/catalog/ai diet-safety issues first (for this app, a banned-food or misapplied-Floor-Day defect risks physical harm to a UC patient, so I ranked those alongside — and in the critical tier, ahead of — plain data-loss/sync defects), then health/expenditure data-correctness, then export/tests-ci operational hygiene, then UI last.

Grouping rationale: findings were merged into one branch only when they shared a literal root cause (the same word list, the same lock table, the same wiring gap) or the same function/file region (e.g. lib/store.ts:158 for findings 4+10, ProposalCard.tsx's decide() for 59+62, Sheet.tsx:68 for 61+88, ci.yml's Supabase-branch gate for 18+52). Findings that merely resemble each other in pattern (e.g. the many separate "no error handling on a network/IndexedDB write" UI bugs in different components) were kept as separate branches since they touch different functions with independent fixes, to keep each branch small enough for a fix agent to land in parallel.

Human-ruling flag: branch 63 (fix-mealcard-design-contract-drift, finding #72) is the only confirmed finding whose proposed fix could involve changing a DESIGN.md-owned token rather than just conforming code to the existing contract — flag this for Devin's sign-off before a fix agent starts, per the design-contract convention that DESIGN.md changes need human ruling. No confirmed finding proposes changing a number owned by ../analysis/targets.md; the closest candidates (finding #13's Floor Day rescaling, finding #20's target-proposal wiring, finding #82's burn-in day threshold) all fix code that reads or applies existing authored numbers rather than editing the source-of-truth file's values, so none were flagged.

Two findings were absorbed into higher-severity groups despite being medium/low themselves because their worst co-finding set the branch severity: #55 (medium) rides with #43/#46 (high) in branch 9; #63 (medium) rides with #15/#32 (high) in branch 30; #75/#80 (medium) ride with #1/#9/#12 (critical) in branch 1; #83/#85/#90/#91 similarly ride with higher-severity partners in branches 35, 36, 11, and 24 respectively.

1. `fix/widen-banned-food-vocabulary` [core-rules] findings #1, #9, #12, #75, #80. All five share the one root cause: the hand-maintained banned-term word lists (bannedReason in lib/foods.ts, and the mirrored containsDairyTerm/containsGlutenTerm in lib/meals.test.ts) are missing common dairy/gluten terms (buttermilk, malt, named cheeses, kefir, orzo, beer, couscous, ghee, whey, casein). One fix agent should build a single shared banned-term source and update both the production gate and its test-vocabulary mirror together.
2. `fix/fix-floor-day-avoid-and-scaling` [core-rules] findings #11, #13. Both are defects in resolveDay's Floor Day branch: it substitutes FLOOR_DAY_MEALS without checking her Avoid list, and separately rescales those meals instead of keeping the documented fixed total. Same function, same code region, fix together.
3. `fix/fix-label-scan-allergen-gaps` [ai] findings #7. AI label-photo capture's own weaker allergen keyword list misses buttermilk/caseinate/milkfat, letting a photographed label through the coach/capture pipeline uncaught.
4. `fix/fix-scan-allergen-fail-open-nonenglish` [catalog] findings #8. scanAllergens (the shared OFF text-scan engine) fails open on non-English or vocabulary-gap ingredient text with no quality flag, a distinct gap from the client-side regex issues below.
5. `fix/fix-catalog-client-offplan-verdict` [catalog] findings #5, #26. Both are in lib/catalog-client.ts's off-plan flagging path: the client drops the server's real policy verdict (5) and its own fallback regex is missing whole gluten words (26) — same function, same fix effort (prefer server verdict, widen the regex as the offline fallback).
6. `fix/wire-today-screen-writes-to-sync-outbox` [sync] findings #4, #10, #24, #25. All four trace to the same lib/store.ts write path: it never enqueues into the new sync outbox (10), silently reverts on localStorage write failure (4, same cited line), the sync status card then falsely reports everything sent (24), and the gut-log control writes a divergent record from what the coach reads (25) — one wiring fix resolves the whole cluster.
7. `fix/fix-legacy-sync-field-merge` [sync] findings #2. The live MCP tools (log_gut, log_food) push whole-record last-write-wins updates through lib/server/db.ts instead of a field-level merge, risking silent data loss on concurrent writes.
8. `fix/fix-logfoodsheet-commit-pipeline` [sync] findings #3, #6. Both are gaps in LogFoodSheet's commit flow: multi-item 'Log a meal' entries never call the server commit at all (3), and a rejected/blocked single-item commit leaves a phantom, uncounted-as-failed entry in her log (6) — same component, same commit pipeline.
9. `fix/fix-server-date-utc-local-mismatch` [core-rules] findings #43, #46, #55. Three separate server surfaces (MCP get_day/coach 'today', ramp-stage/targets activeStage, weekly-review's week_start) all compute the calendar day from server UTC time instead of Sasha's local day — the same missing local-date plumbing, fixable with one shared helper.
10. `fix/fix-rotation-date-purity` [core-rules] findings #22. resolveDay is documented as a pure function of date but silently rewrites already-eaten past days' meals whenever her Avoid/Eats list changes later.
11. `fix/fix-empty-role-meal-slot-fallback` [core-rules] findings #34, #90. A macro role fully marked Avoid silently drops a slot/role in lib/foods.ts's pool selection (34), and the one regression test meant to catch exactly this (lib/rotation.test.ts 'no gaps') is tautological and cannot catch it (90) — fix the production fallback and the test together.
12. `fix/fix-constraints-save-error-handling` [core-rules] findings #36. ConstraintsEditor's save has no error handling, so an allergen/avoid-list edit can silently vanish on a dropped connection — a safety-relevant data-loss case, not just cosmetic.
13. `fix/fix-missing-kcal-atwater-fallback` [catalog] findings #14, #39. Both are the same missing-kcal-fallback bug in different snapshotMacros call sites (log-client.ts's confirm screen, resolve.ts's day totals): a food with real macros but no reported energy_kcal should fall back to the already-computed Atwater energy_kcal_calc instead of showing/counting 0.
14. `fix/thread-category-and-quality-flags-in-barcode` [catalog] findings #33, #38. Both are the same toMatch() function in lib/server/barcode.ts dropping a field it should carry through: category (blocking the simple_sugars banned-category rule) and qualityFlags (the energy_mismatch pill) never reach the client.
15. `fix/fix-food-db-offplan-flag-gaps` [catalog] findings #23, #28. Both are off-plan-flag completeness gaps in lib/food-db.ts: the bundled DB ships wrong/missing flags for known gluten/dairy foods (23) and mineAsDb never computes flags at all for her own added foods (28).
16. `fix/fix-coach-draft-verdict-loss` [ai] findings #29. draft_log_entry's real per-item policy verdict is computed then discarded; the confirm sheet always renders 'allowed' regardless of the true check.
17. `fix/fix-coach-log-this-button-binding` [ai] findings #40. CoachChat's 'Log this' button is bound to the shared draft rather than the specific suggestion tapped, so it can log the wrong food from a multi-suggestion reply.
18. `fix/fix-zero-gram-capture-commit` [ai] findings #50. AI-capture confirm can commit a real food at 0 grams/0 kcal with no floor or removal, unlike the equivalent recipe_items check.
19. `fix/log-capture-provider-failures` [ai] findings #51. Capture-path provider failures (photo/voice/text/label) are never logged server-side, unlike the equivalent Coach path that already got this fix.
20. `fix/wire-duplicate-entry-fingerprint-check` [sync] findings #47. entryFingerprint exists but is never called at log-commit time, so the duplicate-detection machinery never actually catches a double-logged meal.
21. `fix/fix-gone-op-result-handling` [sync] findings #35. applyResult silently drops a 'gone' op result instead of re-deriving/re-queuing it, leaving the local mirror permanently out of sync with the server.
22. `fix/fix-synconce-push-error-swallow` [sync] findings #48. A pre-network IndexedDB throw inside drainOutbox/pushOutbox escapes syncOnce's error handling entirely, so the sync status card can't distinguish it from a clean sync.
23. `fix/fix-commitlogentry-unawaited-queue` [sync] findings #49. commitLogEntry returns queue(req) without awaiting it in both branches, so an offline/IDB failure is structurally, not really, handled.
24. `fix/fix-health-dedupe-key-integrity` [health] findings #19, #42, #91. All three are gaps in the same dedupe_key/correction-upsert design (SQL generated column plus lib/health/dedupe.ts): device_label is omitted (19), vendor_updated_at is never populated so the correction-upsert WHERE clause can't fire (42), and type is dropped from the key whenever vendor_sample_id is present (91).
25. `fix/fix-oura-timezone-hardcode` [health] findings #30. lib/oura/map.ts hardcodes tz='UTC' on every Oura sample, shifting sleep/RHR/HRV into the wrong local day.
26. `fix/fix-bodymass-unit-trust` [health] findings #53. rollUpDay trusts a body_mass sample's value is already in canonical kg without re-checking its unit field.
27. `fix/fix-sw-cache-masks-offline-state` [health] findings #16. The service worker's network-first fallback serves a stale cached 200 for /api/ health requests, masking true offline state on the Body dashboard.
28. `fix/fix-watercups-stale-field-read` [health] findings #27. resolveDay/MCP get_day read the retired StoredDay.waterMl field instead of the water_log row that water logging now actually writes to.
29. `fix/fix-morning-weight-save-validation` [health] findings #41. QuickActions' morning-weight Save silently no-ops on invalid input with no visible failure state.
30. `fix/fix-job-runs-lock-lifecycle` [expenditure] findings #15, #32, #63. All three are the same job_runs takeLock/releaseLock mechanism failing in different ways across the cron jobs: weekly-review never releases its lock on failure (15), an error-status lock isn't reclaimable (32), and a crashed 'running' lock has no reaper (63) — one shared lock-lifecycle fix covers all three.
31. `fix/fix-weekly-proposal-missed-week` [expenditure] findings #54. A missed/late weekly-proposal cron run permanently skips that week's check-in because weekStart is derived from wall-clock now rather than a persisted high-water mark.
32. `fix/wire-approved-targets-into-resolveday` [expenditure] findings #20. Approving a weekly target proposal writes a new target_profiles row but resolveDay/ratiosFor never reads it, so nothing on the Today screen actually changes.
33. `fix/wire-coach-expenditure-read` [expenditure] findings #44. The coach's and weekly-review's expenditure read is a permanent stub, never wired to the real latestEstimate() the MCP tool already uses.
34. `fix/fix-tdee-stale-during-held` [expenditure] findings #31. TdeeLines renders a confident stale kcal number during held/blocked estimator status instead of gating on status the way the headline copy already does.
35. `fix/fix-nightly-backup-pagination-and-tables` [export] findings #21, #83. Both are gaps in the same nightly cron backup job: it silently truncates every table read at 1000 rows with no pagination (21), and its TABLES list is missing media_objects entirely (83).
36. `fix/fix-export-pagination-truncation` [export] findings #45, #65, #85. All three are silent-truncation bugs across the export subsystem's different kinds: meals CSV drops its cursor and truncates at 5000 rows (45), the weight export paginates by offset with no tie-breaker (65), and the progress export's truncation detection is based on an unenforced per-date row-count assumption (85).
37. `fix/provision-ci-integration-test-branch` [tests-ci] findings #18, #52. Both trace to the same root cause: CI has no Supabase test-branch env vars, so the RLS/cross-user suite (18) and dozens of other correctness suites gated the same way (52) are silently skipped on every push.
38. `fix/fix-install-prompt-reminder-claim` [ui] findings #17. The install banner falsely tells Sasha that installing turns on meal reminders, when reminders actually require a separate Settings step.
39. `fix/fix-recipe-save-error-handling` [ui] findings #37. saveRecipe has no try/catch or finally, so a rejected save leaves the Save button stuck on 'Saving...' forever with no retry path.
40. `fix/fix-spoofable-xff-ratelimit-key` [server-auth] findings #56. Roughly 11 routes key their rate limiter off a client-spoofable X-Forwarded-For header instead of x-real-ip, letting an attacker bypass throttling for free.
41. `fix/fix-oura-callback-ratelimit-key` [server-auth] findings #81. The Oura OAuth callback's rate limit uses one global key shared by every caller, so an unauthenticated flood can lock out Sasha's own real OAuth redirect.
42. `fix/harden-legacy-sync-push-endpoint` [sync] findings #57. The legacy /api/sync push route (still live per AGENTS.md) has no rate limit, size cap, or schema validation on its request body.
43. `fix/fix-resolvefoodref-error-message` [sync] findings #68. resolveFoodRef misreports a transient DB/RPC failure as 'not signed in,' giving Sasha a wrong and non-actionable error message while already authenticated.
44. `fix/fix-offline-scan-cache-verdict-downgrade` [catalog] findings #64. The offline barcode-scan cache always downgrades a previously-blocked verdict to a generic 'unverified' warning instead of persisting and reconstructing the real policy verdict.
45. `fix/fix-catalog-search-plural-matching` [catalog] findings #69. The bundled offline catalog search misses plain plural queries like 'eggs' against singular USDA entries.
46. `fix/fix-notesheet-offplan-line` [catalog] findings #70. NoteSheet drops the specific off-plan allergen line for catalog (Phase 2) logged entries, the one place she goes to read details about a flagged item.
47. `fix/fix-capturesheet-savedraft-failure` [ai] findings #58. CaptureSheet ignores saveDraft's 'too_large'/'unavailable' return value and proceeds as if the draft persisted.
48. `fix/harden-ai-adapter-guardrails` [ai] findings #60, #94. Both are guardrails in lib/ai/adapter.ts that are weaker than documented: the SDK version pin is only enforced at build time, never at runtime (60), and the spend/capture daily caps are check-then-act rather than atomic (94).
49. `fix/fix-circuit-breaker-serverless-state` [ai] findings #71. The AI provider circuit breaker keeps its failure counter in module-level memory, which resets on every fresh serverless instance and never actually trips.
50. `fix/fix-voicecapture-keyboard-and-errors` [ai] findings #73. VoiceCapture has no keyboard-operable path and conflates a denied mic permission with an unsupported device in its error messaging.
51. `fix/fix-ai-canary-coach-model-probe` [ai] findings #87. ai-canary only probes the text-to-items model, never the Coach's own separately-pinned model, so a Coach-only outage reports as healthy.
52. `fix/fix-health-token-clipboard-feedback` [health] findings #86. The health ingest token sheet's 'Copy it now' action has no failure feedback if the clipboard write silently rejects.
53. `fix/fix-hae-basal-energy-invariant` [health] findings #92. Health Auto Export can write basal_energy samples through an unvalidated path, contradicting the documented Oura-only invariant enforced elsewhere.
54. `fix/fix-proposalcard-error-handling` [expenditure] findings #59, #62. Both are the same decide() function in ProposalCard.tsx: it has no catch for a network failure (59) or for a stale-proposal 409 (62), leaving either failure silent.
55. `fix/fix-targets-fetch-error-state` [expenditure] findings #77. Settings' failed targets fetch resolves to the same null value as a legitimate 'no active targets yet' state, so a real error is indistinguishable from nothing-to-show.
56. `fix/fix-burnin-graduation-unlatch` [expenditure] findings #82. A logging gap longer than the fixed history window re-derives qualifiedTotal from scratch, un-latching an already-graduated burn-in status.
57. `fix/add-backup-staleness-alerting` [export] findings #84. The real (retained) Mac backup script has no failure or staleness alerting if it stops running silently.
58. `fix/fix-lint-ci-max-warnings` [tests-ci] findings #74. The lint CI step can never fail because eslint-config-next rules are warn-level with no --max-warnings enforcement.
59. `fix/add-down-migration-test-coverage` [tests-ci] findings #93. Six down migrations have zero test coverage of any kind, unlike the pattern already used for other rollback scripts.
60. `fix/fix-sheet-modal-focus-and-scroll` [ui] findings #61, #88. Both are the same Sheet.tsx component missing standard modal behavior: no focus trap or Escape-to-close (61), and no background scroll lock (88).
61. `fix/fix-water-log-error-handling` [ui] findings #66. The water-logging FAB action has no error handling, so a failed IndexedDB write vanishes silently.
62. `fix/fix-favorites-strip-idb-failure` [ui] findings #67. FavoritesStrip vanishes silently on an IndexedDB read failure instead of distinguishing 'no favorites' from 'failed to load.'
63. `fix/fix-mealcard-design-contract-drift` [ui] findings #72. NEEDS HUMAN RULING: meal cards render as a plain shell instead of DESIGN.md's specified charcoal/olive meal-card recipe. The finding's own proposed fix has two branches — apply the existing DESIGN.md tokens, or update DESIGN.md itself and delete the unused CSS if the flatter card is now intended — and the second path changes a DESIGN.md token, which per project convention needs Devin's sign-off before a fix agent touches it.
64. `fix/fix-coachchat-message-state-a11y` [ui] findings #76, #78. Both are the same CoachChat.tsx message-list state handling: a failed turn leaves a permanent ghost 'sending' bubble (76), and new assistant replies are never announced via aria-live the way the transient 'Thinking' state already is (78).
65. `fix/fix-tabbar-body-capability-jump` [ui] findings #79. The tab bar's column count jumps once the async Body capability check resolves after first paint.
66. `fix/fix-weekgrid-missed-day-indicator` [ui] findings #89. WeekGrid renders a missed day and a not-yet-due day with the same neutral grey dot, defeating the at-a-glance purpose of the view.
67. `fix/fix-media-upload-url-expiry` [server-auth] findings #97. The signed media upload URL stays valid far longer than the 60 seconds the app's own response claims.
68. `fix/fix-duplicate-groups-upsert-race` [sync] findings #96. duplicate_groups' upsert only guards the id primary key, not the (user_id, fingerprint) unique constraint two devices can race to insert against.
69. `fix/wire-catalog-cache-eviction` [catalog] findings #98. The offline catalog-cache eviction function exists but is dead code, so stale scan snapshots never expire.
70. `fix/fix-status-bar-contrast` [ui] findings #95. The PWA's black-translucent status bar style renders unreadable white icons over the light NeuForm ground in standalone mode.
